. This reduction of structure group is equivalent to the existence of a hermitian metric, called
harmonic metric
on
E_{K}
which induces a decomposition of the flat connection
\nabla
with respect to the Cartan decomposition of the Lie algebra
\mathfrak{g}=\mathfrak{k}\oplus\mathfrak{m}
,

(9.3)

\displaystyle\nabla=D+\Psi,

where
D
is a
K
-connection on
E_{K}
and
\Psi\in\mathcal{A}^{1}(E_{K}(\mathfrak{m}))
.
Note that
\mathfrak{m}
is canonically isomorphic to
T_{x}\mathcal{X}
for the unique
x\in\mathcal{X}
such that
\mathrm{Stab}_{G}(x)=K
, which we call the
standard point
in
\mathcal{X}
. The harmonicity of the
\rho
-equivariant map
f_{\mathcal{X}}
is equivalent to the fact that this is a critical point of the energy functional

E(f_{\mathcal{X}})=\int_{X}|df_{\mathcal{X}}|^{2}\mathrm{dvol}_{h_{0}},

equivalently the map
f_{\mathcal{X}}
is a solution of the
Euler–Lagrange equation

(9.4)

d_{\nabla_{\mathrm{LC}}}^{*}df_{\mathcal{X}}=0,

where
df_{\mathcal{X}}\in\Gamma(T^{*}X\otimes f_{\mathcal{X}}^{*}T\mathcal{X})=\mathcal{A}^{1}(f_{\mathcal{X}}^{*}T\mathcal{X})
is the differential of the map
f_{\mathcal{X}}
, and
d_{\nabla_{\mathrm{LC}}}
is the exterior covariant derivative

d_{\nabla_{\mathrm{LC}}}\colon\mathcal{A}^{k}(f_{\mathcal{X}}^{*}T\mathcal{X})\to\mathcal{A}^{k+1}(f_{\mathcal{X}}^{*}T\mathcal{X})

induced by the pull-back Levi-Civita connection
\nabla_{\mathrm{LC}}:=f_{\mathcal{X}}^{*}\nabla_{T\mathcal{X}}
on the tangent bundle
T\mathcal{X}
of the symmetric space
\mathcal{X}
. More explicitly,

d_{\nabla_{\mathrm{LC}}}(\alpha\otimes s)=d\alpha\otimes s+(-1)^{k}\alpha\otimes\nabla_{\mathrm{LC}}(s).

Remember that for the standard point
x
in
\mathcal{X}
, we have canonically identified
\mathfrak{m}
with
T_{x}\mathcal{X}
. Then, the complex bundle
E_{K}(T_{x}\mathcal{X})
is identified with
E_{K}(\mathfrak{m})
as a principal bundle over
X
with fiber
\mathfrak{m}
. On the other hand, the pull-back bundle
f^{*}_{\mathcal{X}}T\mathcal{X}
is also a well-defined complex bundle over
\tilde{X}
. This bundle descends to a principal bundle over the surface
X
: The existence of a
\rho
-equivariant map
f
means that there is a reduction of the structure group of
E_{G}
, in other words, there is an isomorphism of
G
-bundles
\iota\colon E_{K}\times_{K}G\to E_{G}
. Indeed,
f
corresponds to a section
h\in\Gamma(X,E_{G}/K)
, which gives rise to
E_{K}
as the pullback bundle
h^{*}(E_{G})
over
X
.

We then have the following identification of
K
-bundles over
X
:

Proposition 9.5
.

Let
f\colon\tilde{X}\to\mathcal{X}=G/K
be a
\rho
-equivariant map. Then there is an identification of complex bundles over
\tilde{X}

E_{K}(\mathfrak{m})\cong f^{*}_{\mathcal{X}}T\mathcal{X},

which is equivariant under the action of
\pi_{1}(X)
.
Under this identification, we have that
\Psi=df_{\mathcal{X}}
.

Before we get into the proof of this proposition, we need the following result about a certain correspondence between differential forms over fiber bundles.

Let
\mathrm{pr}\colon E\to M
be a principal
H
-bundle over a smooth manifold
M
and let
R_{g}\colon E\to E
be the action of an element
g\in H
on
E
. The tangent vectors in the kernel of
d\mathrm{pr}\colon TE\to TM
are called
vertical
.

Now let
V
be a vector space and
\rho\colon H\to\mathrm{GL}(V)
be a homomorphism. Let
E(V)
be the corresponding associate bundle. We remind that a form
\omega\in\Omega^{1}(E,V)
is called
horizontal
if the kernel of
\omega
contains all vertical vectors. A form
\omega
is
\rho
-equivariant if for all
g\in H
and all
p\in E
,
v\in T_{p}E
,

\omega_{R(g)p}(dR_{g}(v))=\rho(g^{-1})\omega_{p}(v).

We have the following

Lemma 9.6
.

There is a canonical 1-1 correspondence between horizontal,
\rho
-equivariant forms on
E
and all
V
-valued forms on
M
.

Proof of Lemma
9.6
.

For
p\in E
,
v\in T_{p}E
, and
\omega\in\Omega^{1}(E,V)
, let

\alpha^{\omega}_{\mathrm{pr}(p)}(d\mathrm{pr}(v)):=[(p,\omega_{p}(v))].

The definition of
\alpha^{\omega}
above does not depend on any of the choices made. Indeed, let
p_{1},p_{2}\in E
be such that
p_{2}:=R(g)p_{1}
for a point
g\in H
, and let
v_{1}\in T_{p_{1}}E
,
v_{2}\in T_{p_{2}}E
be such that

d\mathrm{pr}(v_{1})=d\mathrm{pr}(v_{2})\in T_{\mathrm{pr}(g_{1})}M.

Then, we see that

\displaystyle\alpha^{\omega}_{p_{2}}(d\mathrm{pr}(v_{2}))

\displaystyle=[p_{2},\omega_{p_{2}}(v_{2})]

\displaystyle=[p_{1}g,\rho(g^{-1})\omega_{p_{1}}(dR_{g^{-1}}v_{2})]

\displaystyle=[p_{1},\omega_{g_{1}}(v_{1})]

\displaystyle=\alpha^{\omega}_{p_{1}}(d\mathrm{pr}(v_{1})).

In the last two steps, we used that the form is
H
-equivariant and then that it is horizontal.

Conversely, for a form
\alpha\in\Omega^{1}(M,P\times_{H}V)
, we define a horizontal and
\rho
-equivariant element
\omega^{\alpha}\in\Omega^{1}(E,V)
as follows: for
p\in E
and
v\in T_{p}E
, then
w:=\omega^{\alpha}_{p}(v)\in V
is the (unique) element in
V
such that

[p,w]=\alpha_{\mathrm{pr}(p)}(d\mathrm{pr}(v)).

This form is
\rho
-equivariant, since for any
g\in H
, we have that

[R(g)p,\rho(g^{-1})w]=[p,w].

In particular,

\omega^{\alpha}_{R(g)p}(dR_{g}(v))=\rho(g^{-1})\omega^{\alpha}_{p}(v).

Moreover, it is horizontal, because by the definition of
\omega^{\alpha}(v)
, it depends only on the projection of
v\in TE
to
TM
, i.e. evaluation on vertical directions always gives zero.
∎

We now apply Lemma
9.6
for the case when
E=G
,
H=K
,
M=\mathcal{X}
,
V=\mathfrak{m}
and
\rho=\mathrm{Ad}
, the adjoint action of
K
restricted to
\mathfrak{m}
; this will then give a proof of Proposition
9.5
:

Proof of Proposition
9.5
.

Note first that
G\to\mathcal{X}
is a
K
-bundle, thus the pull-back bundle
f_{\mathcal{X}}^{*}G
has a structure of
K
-bundle over
\tilde{X}
, and it descends to a
K
-bundle over
X
, which is exactly the
K
-reduction
E_{K}
of
E_{G}
. Denote by
G(\mathfrak{m}):=G\times_{\mathrm{Ad}(K)}\mathfrak{m}
, and by
G(\mathfrak{g}/\mathfrak{k}):=G\times_{\mathrm{Ad}(K)}\mathfrak{g}/\mathfrak{k}
the associate bundle over
\mathcal{X}
, which can be identified with
T\mathcal{X}

[
8
, Section 1]
, since

(9.7)

T\mathcal{X}\cong G(\mathfrak{g}/\mathfrak{k})\cong G(\mathfrak{m}).

More precisely, an element of
G(\mathfrak{g}/\mathfrak{k})
can be identified with the equivalence class of a pair
[g,v+\mathfrak{k}]
with
g\in G
,
v\in\mathfrak{g}
such that
(g,v+\mathfrak{k})\sim(gk,Ad(k^{-1})v+\mathfrak{k})
, for all
k\in K
.
Similarly, elements of
G(\mathfrak{m})
are the equivalence classes of pairs
[g,m]
with
g\in G
,
m\in\mathfrak{m}
such that
(g,m)\sim(gk,Ad(k^{-1})m)
, for all
k\in K
.

The map

\displaystyle\Phi\colon G(\mathfrak{m})

\displaystyle\to G(\mathfrak{g}/\mathfrak{k})

\displaystyle[g,m]

\displaystyle\mapsto[g,m+\mathfrak{k}]

is a
G
-equivariant isomorphism between bundles. Indeed, one checks that

\displaystyle\Phi([gk,Ad(k^{-1})m])

\displaystyle=[gk,Ad(k^{-1})m+\mathfrak{k}]

\displaystyle=[g,m+\mathfrak{k}]

\displaystyle=\Phi([g,m]),

thus
\Phi
is well-defined. Furthermore,
\Phi
is an isomorphism because
\mathfrak{m}
is a complement of
\mathfrak{k}
in
\mathfrak{g}
.

Finally, the space
T\mathcal{X}
can be identified with the space of equivalence classes of pairs
[g,v+Ad(g)\mathfrak{k}]
with
g\in G
,
v\in\mathfrak{g}
such that
(g,v+Ad(g)\mathfrak{k})\sim(gk,v+Ad(g)\mathfrak{k})
. We identify
T\mathcal{X}
and
G(\mathfrak{g}/\mathfrak{k})
using the bundle isomorphism

\displaystyle\Phi^{\prime}\colon T\mathcal{X}

\displaystyle\to G(\mathfrak{g}/\mathfrak{k}),

\displaystyle[g,v+Ad(g)\mathfrak{k}]

\displaystyle\mapsto[g,Ad(g^{-1})v+\mathfrak{k}].

This map is also well-defined since

\displaystyle\Phi^{\prime}([gk,v+Ad(g)\mathfrak{k}])

\displaystyle=[gk,Ad((gk)^{-1})v+\mathfrak{k}]

\displaystyle=[gk,Ad(k^{-1})Ad(g^{-1})v+\mathfrak{k}]

\displaystyle=[g,Ad(g^{-1})v+\mathfrak{k}]

\displaystyle=\Phi^{\prime}([g,v+Ad(g)\mathfrak{k}]).

Now since the action of
G
on
T\mathcal{X}
is described by

(g^{\prime},[g,v+Ad(g)\mathfrak{k}])\mapsto[g^{\prime}g,Ad(g^{\prime})v+Ad(g^{\prime}g)\mathfrak{k}],

we can define the action of
G
on the spaces
G(\mathfrak{g}/\mathfrak{k})
and
G(\mathfrak{m})
by

(g^{\prime},[g,v+\mathfrak{k}])\mapsto[g^{\prime}g,v+\mathfrak{k}]\ \mathrm{and}\ (g^{\prime},[g,m])\mapsto[g^{\prime}g,m],

respectively.

The maps
\Phi
and
\Phi^{\prime}
are both equivariant with respect to these two actions. Indeed, one sees that

\displaystyle\Phi^{\prime}(g^{\prime}.[g,v+Ad(g)\mathfrak{k}])

\displaystyle=\Phi^{\prime}([g^{\prime}g,Ad(g^{\prime})v+Ad(g^{\prime}g)\mathfrak{k}])

\displaystyle=[g^{\prime}g,Ad(g^{-1})v+\mathfrak{k}]

\displaystyle=g^{\prime}.[g,Ad(g^{-1})v+\mathfrak{k}]

\displaystyle=g.\Phi^{\prime}([g,v+Ad(g)\mathfrak{k}]).

Thus, the bundle
E_{K}(\mathfrak{m})\to X
is now defined by taking the quotient of the pull-back bundle
f^{*}_{\mathcal{X}}(G(\mathfrak{m}))\to\tilde{X}
by the action of the group of deck transformations on
\tilde{X}
. Similarly, the bundle
E_{K}(\mathfrak{g}/\mathfrak{k})\to X
is defined as the quotient of the pull-back bundle
f^{*}_{\mathcal{X}}(G(\mathfrak{g}/\mathfrak{k}))\to\tilde{X}
by the image of the representation
\rho
in
G
. Then, the equivariant isomorphisms
\Phi
and
\Phi^{\prime}
described earlier induce a bundle isomorphism

E_{K}(\mathfrak{m})\cong f^{*}_{\mathcal{X}}T\mathcal{X},

which is equivariant under the action of
\pi_{1}(X)
.

Now, consider the
K
-bundle given by the natural projection
\mathrm{pr}\colon G\to G/K=\mathcal{X}
and remember the Maurer–Cartan form
\omega^{MC}\in\Omega^{1}(G,\mathfrak{g})
given by the differential of the left-multiplication map
(L_{g^{-1}})_{*}\colon T_{g}G\to\mathfrak{g}
. Then, the bundle identification

\omega_{\mathfrak{m}}:=\Phi^{-1}\circ\,\Phi^{\prime}\colon TG/K\to G\times_{\mathrm{Ad}(K)}\mathfrak{m}

can be seen as an element in

\Omega^{1}(G/K,G\times_{\mathrm{Ad}(K)}\mathfrak{m}),

which is naturally identified with the subspace of horizontal,
Ad(K)
-equivariant forms of
\Omega^{1}(G,\mathfrak{m})
by Lemma
9.6
.

It then follows that the form
\omega_{K}:=\omega^{MC}-\omega_{\mathfrak{m}}\in\Omega^{1}(G,\mathfrak{k})
. The space
\Omega^{1}(G,\mathfrak{k})
can be naturally identified with
\Omega^{1}(P_{K},\mathfrak{k})
, where
P_{K}
is the following
K
-subbundle of the trivial
G
-bundle
G/K\times G\to G/K
:

P_{K}=\{(gK,gk)\mid g\in G,\;k\in K\}\subset G/K\times G\to G/K,

and
G\overset{\cong}{\longrightarrow}P_{K}
with
g\mapsto(\mathrm{pr}(g),g)
, where
\mathrm{pr}\colon G\to G/K
is the natural projection as above.

Finally, having a
\rho
-equivariant map
f_{\mathcal{X}}\colon\tilde{X}\to G/K
, one obtains the bundle
f_{\mathcal{X}}^{*}(G/K\times G)=E_{G}
equipped with the flat connection
\nabla=f_{\mathcal{X}}^{*}\omega^{MC}
, and the
K
-subbundle
f^{*}(P_{K})\subset E_{G}
with a connection
D=f_{\mathcal{X}}^{*}\omega_{K}
(which is in general non flat). Then,

\Psi:=\nabla-D=f_{\mathcal{X}}^{*}\omega_{\mathfrak{m}}=\omega_{\mathfrak{m}}\circ df.

Under the identification
TG/K\cong G\times_{\mathrm{Ad}(K)}\mathfrak{m}
which is, as we have seen above, provided by
\omega_{\mathfrak{m}}
, we can identify
\Psi
with
df
(as is usually seen in the literature in the case when
G=\mathrm{GL}_{n}(\mathbb{C})
).
∎

Remark 9.8
.

The unitary connection
D
above is essentially the pull-back of the Levi-Civita connection of
\mathcal{X}
; this connection does not depend on the choice of the model
\mathcal{X}
.

Now let
d_{\nabla_{\mathrm{LC}}}
be the exterior covariant derivative of the pull-back Levi-Civita connection
\nabla_{\mathrm{LC}}
, then the harmonicity condition on the map
f_{\mathcal{X}}
implies that the following three equations are satisfied:

(9.9)

\displaystyle(\bar{\partial}_{\nabla_{\mathrm{LC}}})^{2}

\displaystyle=0,

(9.10)

\displaystyle\bar{\partial}_{\nabla_{\mathrm{LC}}}d^{1,0}f_{\chi}

\displaystyle=0,

(9.11)

\displaystyle[d^{1,0}f_{\chi},d^{1,0}f_{\chi}]

\displaystyle=0,

where
d_{\nabla_{\mathrm{LC}}}
decomposes into types as

d_{\nabla_{\mathrm{LC}}}=\partial_{\nabla_{\mathrm{LC}}}+\bar{\partial}_{\nabla_{\mathrm{LC}}}.

Consider the
K^{\mathbb{C}}
-bundle
E_{K^{\mathbb{C}}}
obtained from
E_{K}
by extension of structure group
K\subset K^{\mathbb{C}}
. Take also the complexification of the Cartan decomposition of the Lie algebra

\mathfrak{g}^{\mathbb{C}}=\mathfrak{k}^{\mathbb{C}}\oplus\mathfrak{m}^{\mathbb{C}}.

Then, the connection
\nabla
on the flat complex bundle
E_{G}
decomposes as

\nabla=D+\Psi,

for
D
a
K^{\mathbb{C}}
-connection on
E_{K^{\mathbb{C}}}
and
\Psi\in\mathcal{A}^{1}(E_{K^{\mathbb{C}}}(\mathfrak{m}^{\mathbb{C}}))
. Taking complexification we further have
\mathfrak{m}^{\mathbb{C}}\cong T_{x}^{\mathbb{C}}\mathcal{X}
and moreover

E_{K^{\mathbb{C}}}(T_{x}^{\mathbb{C}}\mathcal{X})\cong f^{*}_{\mathcal{X}}T^{\mathbb{C}}\mathcal{X},

for the complexified tangent bundle
T^{\mathbb{C}}\mathcal{X}
of the symmetric space
\mathcal{X}
.

For the complexified version of the harmonicity equations, a (complexified) solution on the space
\mathcal{A}^{\bullet}(f^{*}_{\mathcal{X}}T^{\mathbb{C}}\mathcal{X})
gives a holomorphic structure
\bar{\partial}_{\nabla_{\mathrm{LC}}}
. Now, taking
\bar{\partial}_{E}:=\bar{\partial}_{\nabla_{\mathrm{LC}}}
and
\varphi:=\Psi^{1,0}=d^{1,0}f_{\chi}
these equations (
9.9
)-(
9.11
) are rewritten as

(9.12)

\displaystyle(\bar{\partial}_{E})^{2}

\displaystyle=0,

(9.13)

\displaystyle\bar{\partial}_{E}\varphi

\displaystyle=0,

(9.14)

\displaystyle[\varphi,\varphi]

\displaystyle=0.

Note that over a compact Riemann surface
X
, equations (
9.12
) and (
9.14
) are automatically satisfied.

In summary, a reductive fundamental group representation

\rho\colon\pi_{1}(X)\to G
determines a solution of the equation
\bar{\partial}_{E}\varphi=0
. More precisely, it determines a holomorphic structure
\bar{\partial}_{E}
, and a section
\varphi
of
\mathcal{A}^{1}(f^{*}_{\mathcal{X}}T^{\mathbb{C}}\mathcal{X})
which is holomorphic with respect to
\bar{\partial}_{E}
.

The obtained triple is denoted as
(E_{K^{\mathbb{C}}},\bar{\partial}_{E},\varphi)
; we claim it is polystable as in the classical case. Indeed, let
\sigma:X\to E_{K^{\mathbb{C}}}/K
be the
K
-reduction mentioned above, then there is a unique connection
\nabla_{A}
that is compatible with both the holomorphic structure
\bar{\partial}_{E}
and the reduction
\sigma
, which is called the
Chern connection
. Let

\tau:\mathcal{A}^{1,0}(E_{K^{\mathbb{C}}}(\mathfrak{m}^{\mathbb{C}}))\to\mathcal{A}^{0,1}(E_{K^{\mathbb{C}}}(\mathfrak{m}^{\mathbb{C}}))

be the conjugation on
\mathcal{A}^{1,0}(E_{K^{\mathbb{C}}}(\mathfrak{m}^{\mathbb{C}}))
defined by the reduction
\sigma
and the standard conjugation on
X
from
(1,0)
-forms to
(0,1)
-forms, then
-\tau(\varphi)=d^{0,1}f
and
\nabla_{A}+\varphi-\tau(\varphi)
is a
G
-connection on the
G
-bundle
\sigma^{*}E_{K^{\mathbb{C}}}\times_{K}G\cong E_{G}
, which is indeed the original flat connection
\nabla
. Therefore,
(E_{K^{\mathbb{C}}},\bar{\partial}_{E},\varphi)
satisfies the following Hitchin’s equation

\displaystyle F_{A}-[\varphi,\tau(\varphi)]=0.

This implies that the obtained triple
(E_{K^{\mathbb{C}}},\bar{\partial}_{E},\varphi)
is polystable (of degree
0
).

The previous discussion motivates the following:

Definition 9.15
.

Let
X
be a compact Riemann surface of genus
g\geq 2
and let
G
be a reductive Lie group. Fix a reductive fundamental group representation

\rho\colon\pi_{1}(X)\to G
corresponding to a flat principal
G
-bundle
E_{G}
. Consider a
\rho
-equivariant
map
f_{\mathcal{X}}\colon\tilde{X}\to\mathcal{X}
defined over the universal cover
\tilde{X}
of
X
. The map
f_{\mathcal{X}}
determines a reduction
E_{K}
of structure group of
E_{G}
from
G
to a maximal compact subgroup
K
of
G
. A
polystable
G
-Higgs bundle
over
X
is a pair
(\mathcal{E},\varphi)
where
\mathcal{E}:=(E_{K^{\mathbb{C}}},\bar{\partial}_{E_{K^{\mathbb{C}}}})
is a holomorphic principal
K^{\mathbb{C}}
-bundle over
X
and
\varphi
is a
(1,0)
-form with values in
f^{*}_{\mathcal{X}}T^{\mathbb{C}}\mathcal{X}
, such that
\bar{\partial}_{E_{K^{\mathbb{C}}}}\varphi=0
.

Remark 9.16
.

Note that the bundle
f^{*}_{\mathcal{X}}T^{\mathbb{C}}\mathcal{X}
above is defined over the universal cover
\tilde{X}
of
X
. Moreover, from the preceding analysis, given a
reductive
fundamental group representation, Corlette’s theorem for Hadamard manifolds reviewed in Section
9.1
, ensures the existence of a
\rho
-equivariant map
f_{\mathcal{X}}\colon\tilde{X}\to\mathcal{X}
determining a reduction of structure group of the bundle
E_{G}
. For a copy of the symmetric space
\mathcal{X}
viewed as the Riemannian manifold
G/K
and using the identification
E_{K}(\mathfrak{m}^{\mathbb{C}})\cong f^{*}_{\mathcal{X}}T^{\mathbb{C}}\mathcal{X}
provided by Proposition
9.5
, then Definition
9.15
reduces to the classical definition of a polystable
G
-Higgs bundle.

Remark 9.17
.

Definition
9.15
is obtained starting from a fixed reductive fundamental group representation
\rho
, thus we call the resulting
G
-Higgs bundle
polystable
. A more general definition of
G
-Higgs bundles which does not depend on a choice of reductive representation is presented in Appendix
A
. However, for the rest of the paper we study explicitly only the polystable pairs in the sense of Definition
9.15
for the various models
\mathcal{X}
of the symmetric space.

A moduli space of polystable
G
-Higgs bundles in terms of Definition
9.15
can be obtained using the theory of Schmitt
[
18
, Section 2.7]
for constructing moduli spaces of principal
G
-bundles with sections in an associated bundle. This is due to the fact that the model we choose for defining the Higgs fields does not affect the algebraic structure. We will denote this moduli space by
\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{Higgs}}(X,G)
.

10.
Higgs bundle data

In this Section we illustrate descriptions of Higgs bundle data for all families of Lie groups we are studying in this article. We describe explicitly the data for the groups
\mathrm{O}_{(1,1)}(A,\sigma)
and
\mathrm{Sp}_{2}(A,\sigma)
in all four models of the symmetric space. Since the groups
A^{\times}
and
\mathrm{O}(A_{\mathbb{C}},\sigma_{\mathbb{C}})
can be both embedded as subgroups into
\mathrm{O}_{(1,1)}(A,\sigma)
, the
G
-Higgs bundle data in these cases can be induced as special cases of
\mathrm{O}_{(1,1)}(A,\sigma)
-data. For the groups
\mathrm{Sp}_{2}(A_{\mathbb{C}},\sigma_{\mathbb{C}})
, we demonstrate the Higgs bundle data only in the quaternionic structures model which is the most interesting case.

10.1.
Higgs bundles for models associated to groups
\mathrm{O}_{(1,1)}(A,\sigma)

For an indefinite orthogonal group
\mathrm{O}_{(1,1)}(A,\sigma)
over a Hermitian noncommutative algebra
A
with an anti-involution
\sigma
, we are now ready to provide various different interpretations of the pairs of Definition
9.15
in terms of each of the four models of the symmetric space
\mathcal{X}:=\mathcal{X}_{\mathrm{O}_{(1,1)}(A,\sigma)}
described explicitly in Section
3
.

Since
\mathrm{O}_{(1,1)}(A,\sigma)
is a noncompact reductive Lie group, if
(A,\sigma)
is a Hermitian algebra, the symmetric space
\mathcal{X}
is always a Hadamard manifold, thus we can apply Corlette’s theorem from Section
9.1
. Note that the holomorphic structure
\bar{\partial}_{E}:=\bar{\partial}_{D}=\bar{\partial}_{\nabla_{\mathrm{LC}}}
depends only on
D
and not on the model of
\mathcal{X}
we choose. Thus, it is only the Higgs field
\varphi
and the condition
\bar{\partial}_{E}\varphi=0
that receive an alternative interpretation depending on the model
\mathcal{X}
considered.

Let
\rho\colon\pi_{1}(X)\to\mathrm{O}_{(1,1)}(A,\sigma)
be a reductive representation. By the Riemann–Hilbert correspondence,
\rho
corresponds to a flat
\mathrm{O}_{(1,1)}(A,\sigma)
-bundle, namely, an
A^{2}
-bundle with the standard indefinite orthogonal form on
A^{2}

\omega(x,y):=\sigma(x)^{t}\Omega y,

where
\Omega=\begin{pmatrix}0&1\\
1&0\end{pmatrix}
, for
x,y\in A^{2}
. Let us denote by
(E,\omega,\nabla)
this flat bundle.

In order to describe the
G=\mathrm{O}_{(1,1)}(A,\sigma)
-Higgs bundle data for each choice of a model of the symmetric space, we need to specify the holomorphic
K^{\mathbb{C}}
-bundle and the corresponding Higgs field explicitly, for
K=\mathrm{KO}_{(1,1)}(A,\sigma)
. Note that given a
\rho
-equivariant harmonic map
f\colon\tilde{X}\to\mathcal{X}
, we consider the
K^{\mathbb{C}}
-bundle over
\tilde{X}
whose fiber over
x\in\tilde{X}
is
(\mathrm{Stab}_{G}(f(x)))^{\mathbb{C}}<\mathrm{O}_{(1,1)}(A_{\mathbb{C}},\sigma_{\mathbb{C}})
. This bundle does not depend on the choice of a model, but only on the map
f
. Whenever we choose a model, we give a geometric description of the object that needs to be stabilized by
\mathrm{Stab}_{G}(f(x))
. Then, the actual difference among the definitions of polystable
G
-Higgs bundles for the four models is reflected on the values the Higgs field takes.

For
\rho\colon\pi_{1}(X)\to\mathrm{O}_{(1,1)}(A,\sigma)
a reductive representation and
f\colon\tilde{X}\to\mathcal{X}
the corresponding
\rho
-equivariant harmonic map, a polystable
\mathrm{O}_{(1,1)}(A,\sigma)
-Higgs bundle is a pair
(E,\varphi)
where
E
is a holomorphic
K^{\mathbb{C}}
-bundle and
\varphi\in H^{0}(X,f^{*}T^{\mathbb{C}}\mathcal{X}\otimes K_{X})
is a holomorphic section. We describe separately the bundle
E
which essentially does not depend on a choice of the model of
\mathcal{X}
and the Higgs field which can be interpreted differently for every model of
\mathcal{X}
.

The holomorphic bundle
E
is defined as follows: For the trivial bundle
\tilde{X}\times\mathrm{O}_{(1,1)}(A_{\mathbb{C}},\sigma_{\mathbb{C}})
, we consider the subbundle
\tilde{E}
whose fiber over
x\in\tilde{X}
is the complexification of the stabilizer of
f(x)\in\mathcal{X}
. Under the natural projection

\tilde{X}\times\mathrm{O}_{(1,1)}(A_{\mathbb{C}},\sigma_{\mathbb{C}})\to(\tilde{X}\times\mathrm{O}_{(1,1)}(A_{\mathbb{C}},\sigma_{\mathbb{C}}))/\rho,

the bundle
\tilde{E}
projects onto a principal
K^{\mathbb{C}}
-subbundle
E
over
X
.

Indefinite involutive operators model.

Recall from Section
3.1
that
\mathfrak{C}_{G}
denotes the indefinite involutive operators model of
\mathcal{X}
. For
x\in X
, let
J_{x}:=f_{\mathfrak{C}_{G}}(x)\in\mathfrak{C}_{G}
be the corresponding indefinite involutive operator. Then we have the identification
\mathrm{Stab}_{G}(J_{x})\cong K
, which acts on the tangent space

T_{J_{x}}\mathfrak{C}_{G}=\{\ L\colon A^{2}\to A^{2}\mid\text{$L$ is $A$-linear and }LJ_{x}+J_{x}L=0\ \};

note that
J_{x}
extends
\mathbb{C}
-linearly to an
A_{\mathbb{C}}
-linear operator
J_{x}^{\mathbb{C}}
on
A_{\mathbb{C}}^{2}
. Therefore, we can give the following description of Higgs bundles in terms of the model
\mathfrak{C}_{G}
:

Definition 10.1
.

A
polystable
G
-Higgs bundle in the indefinite involutive operators model

\mathfrak{C}_{G}
that comes from a reductive representation
\rho\colon\pi_{1}(X)\to G
consists of a holomorphic principal
K^{\mathbb{C}}
-bundle
(E,\bar{\partial}_{E})
together with a
(1,0)
-form
\varphi
with values in
f_{\mathfrak{C}_{G}}^{*}T^{\mathbb{C}}\mathfrak{C}_{G}
, such that
\bar{\partial}_{E}\varphi=0
. More specifically, for each
x\in X
, the fiber
E_{x}
of the principal
K^{\mathbb{C}}
-bundle
E
is
(\mathrm{Stab}_{G}(J_{x}))^{\mathbb{C}}
, and the Higgs field
\varphi(x)
is a
(1,0)
-form with values in

T^{\mathbb{C}}_{J_{x}}\mathfrak{C}_{G}=\{\ L\colon A_{\mathbb{C}}^{2}\to A_{\mathbb{C}}^{2}\mid\text{$L$ is $A_{\mathbb{C}}$-linear and }LJ_{x}^{\mathbb{C}}+J_{x}^{\mathbb{C}}L=0\ \},

such that
\bar{\partial}_{E}\varphi=0
.

Projective space models.

Recall from Section
3.2
that
\mathfrak{P}^{\pm}_{G}
denote the two projective space models of
\mathcal{X}
. For
x\in X
, let
l_{x}:=f_{\mathfrak{P}^{+}_{G}}(x)\in\mathfrak{P}^{+}_{G}
be the corresponding
A
-line in
\mathbb{P}(A^{2})
, and
l_{x}^{\perp_{\omega}}\in\mathfrak{P}_{G}^{-}
the
\omega
-orthogonal line which is always transverse to
l_{x}
. Then we have the identification
\mathrm{Stab}_{G}(l_{x})\cong K
(see Section
9.2
), which acts on the tangent space

T_{l_{x}}\mathfrak{P}^{\pm}_{G}=\mathrm{Hom}(l_{x},A^{2}/l_{x});

note that the
A
-line
l_{x}
extends
\mathbb{C}
-linearly to an
A_{\mathbb{C}}
-line
l_{x}^{\mathbb{C}}\subset A_{\mathbb{C}}^{2}
. Therefore, we can give the following description of Higgs bundles in terms of the model
\mathfrak{P}^{\pm}_{G}
.

As above, for
x\in X
we denote
l_{x}:=f_{\mathfrak{P}_{G}^{+}}(x)\in\mathfrak{P}_{G}^{+}
.

Definition 10.2
.

A
polystable
G
-Higgs bundle in the projective space model

\mathfrak{P}_{G}^{+}
that comes from a reductive representation
\rho\colon\pi_{1}(X)\to G
consists of a holomorphic principal
K^{\mathbb{C}}
-bundle
(E,\bar{\partial}_{E})
together with a
(1,0)
-form
\varphi
with values in
f_{\mathfrak{P}_{G}^{+}}^{*}T^{\mathbb{C}}\mathfrak{P}_{G}^{+}
, such that
\bar{\partial}_{E}\varphi=0
. More specifically, for each
x\in X
, the fiber
E_{x}
of the principal
K^{\mathbb{C}}
-bundle
E
is
(\mathrm{Stab}_{G}(l_{x}))^{\mathbb{C}}
, and the Higgs field
\varphi(x)
is a
(1,0)
-form with values in

T_{l_{x}}^{\mathbb{C}}\mathfrak{P}_{G}^{+}=\mathrm{Hom}(l_{x}^{\mathbb{C}},A_{\mathbb{C}}^{2}/l_{x}^{\mathbb{C}}),

such that
\bar{\partial}_{E}\varphi=0
.

Since the values of the maps
f_{\mathfrak{P}_{G}^{\pm}}
are
A
-lines, i.e.
\mathbb{R}
-vector spaces, and

T_{l_{x}}^{\mathbb{C}}\mathfrak{P}_{G}^{+}=\mathrm{Hom}(l_{x}^{\mathbb{C}},A_{\mathbb{C}}^{2}/l_{x}^{\mathbb{C}})\cong\mathrm{Hom}(l_{x}^{\mathbb{C}},(l_{x}^{\perp_{\omega}})^{\mathbb{C}}),

we can provide an equivalent definition of a polystable
G
-Higgs bundle in the projective space model as follows: we take the flat
G
-bundle defined for a given reductive representation
\rho
by the Riemann-Hilbert correspondence which is a vector bundle
(V_{\mathbb{R}},\nabla)
over
X
with fibers isomorphic to
A^{2}
. We lift this bundle to the universal cover
\tilde{X}
of
X
and denote the lift by
(\tilde{V}_{\mathbb{R}},\tilde{\nabla})
. Corlette’s theorem guarantees the existence of a
\rho
-equivariant harmonic map
f_{\mathfrak{P}_{G}^{+}}
. In terms of the projective space model, this allows us to define a subbundle
\tilde{E}_{\mathbb{R}}
in
\tilde{V}_{\mathbb{R}}
which fiberwise is given by the image of
f_{\mathfrak{P}_{G}^{+}}
, i.e. the fiber over
x\in\tilde{X}
is the
A
-line
f_{\mathfrak{P}_{G}^{+}}(x)\subset A^{2}
. We now complexify the bundle
\tilde{V}_{\mathbb{R}}
and the corresponding subbundle
\tilde{E}_{\mathbb{R}}
. The corresponding complexifications are denoted by
\tilde{V}
and
\tilde{E}
respectively. The flat connection
\nabla
gives rise to a holomorphic structure
\bar{\partial}_{\tilde{E}}
on
\tilde{E}
. Since
f_{\mathfrak{P}_{G}^{+}}
is
\rho
-equivariant, the bundle
(\tilde{E},\bar{\partial}_{\tilde{E}})
projects onto a holomorphic subbundle
(E,\bar{\partial}_{E})
of the bundle
V
over
X
which is the complexification of
V_{\mathbb{R}}
. Since for every
x\in\tilde{X}
, the stabilizer of
f_{\mathfrak{P}_{G}^{+}}(x)
in
G
is isomorphic to
K
, then
(\tilde{E},\bar{\partial}_{\tilde{E}})
is a holomorphic
K^{\mathbb{C}}
-bundle.

Definition 10.3
.

A
polystable
G
-Higgs bundle in the projective space model

\mathfrak{P}_{G}^{+}
that comes from a reductive representation
\rho\colon\pi_{1}(X)\to G
consists of a holomorphic
K^{\mathbb{C}}
-bundle
(E,\bar{\partial}_{E})
defined by
f_{\mathfrak{P}_{G}^{+}}^{\mathbb{C}}
together with a
(1,0)
-form
\varphi
with values in
f_{\mathfrak{P}_{G}^{+}}^{*}T^{\mathbb{C}}\mathfrak{P}_{G}^{+}
, such that
\bar{\partial}_{E}\varphi=0
. More specifically, for each
x\in\tilde{X}
, the fiber over
x
is an
A_{\mathbb{C}}
-line
l_{x}^{\mathbb{C}}:=f_{\mathfrak{P}_{G}^{+}}(x)^{\mathbb{C}}
in
A_{\mathbb{C}}^{2}
, the Higgs field
\varphi(x)
is a
(1,0)
-form with values in

T_{l_{x}}^{\mathbb{C}}\mathfrak{P}_{G}^{+}=\mathrm{Hom}(l_{x}^{\mathbb{C}},A_{\mathbb{C}}^{2}/l_{x}^{\mathbb{C}}),

such that
\bar{\partial}_{E}\varphi=0
.

Equivalently, we can say that the fiber over
x
of a Higgs bundle consists of two
A_{\mathbb{C}}
-lines
l_{x}^{\mathbb{C}}
and
(l_{x}^{\perp_{\omega}})^{\mathbb{C}}
, and a Higgs field a
(1,0)
-form with values in

T_{l_{x}}^{\mathbb{C}}\mathfrak{P}_{G}^{+}=\mathrm{Hom}(l_{x}^{\mathbb{C}},(l_{x}^{\perp_{\omega}})^{\mathbb{C}}),

such that
\bar{\partial}_{E}\varphi=0
.

An analogous definition is obtained for the projective space model
\mathfrak{P}_{G}^{-}
as well.

10.1.1.
Half-space models and precompact model.

Since the half-space models
\mathfrak{U}^{\pm}_{G}
and the precompact model
\mathfrak{B}_{G}
are open domains in
A
, the complexification of the tangent space for every point of
\mathfrak{U}^{\pm}_{G}
and
\mathfrak{B}_{G}
is naturally isomorphic to
A_{\mathbb{C}}
. Therefore,
f^{*}_{\mathfrak{U}^{\pm}_{G}}T^{\mathbb{C}}\mathfrak{U}^{\pm}_{G}
and
f^{*}_{\mathfrak{B}_{G}}T^{\mathbb{C}}\mathfrak{B}_{G}
identify canonically with
\tilde{X}\times A_{\mathbb{C}}
and a Higgs field
\varphi(x)
is in both cases a holomorphic 1-form with values in
A_{\mathbb{C}}
.

The only difference between fibers of
\tilde{X}\times A_{\mathbb{C}}
over different points
x\in\tilde{X}
is the action of the stabilizer
\mathrm{Stab}_{\mathrm{O}_{(1,1)}(A,\sigma)}(f(x))
, which is described in Section
3.3
(for upper half-space model) and Section
3.4
(for precompact model). We thus include here for completeness:

Definition 10.4
.

A
polystable
G
-Higgs bundle in the upper half-space model

\mathfrak{U}^{+}_{G}
or in the
precompact model

\mathfrak{B}_{G}
that comes from a reductive representation
\rho\colon\pi_{1}(X)\to G
, consists of a holomorphic principal
K^{\mathbb{C}}
-bundle
(E,\bar{\partial}_{E})
together with a
(1,0)
-form
\varphi
with values in
f_{\mathfrak{U}^{+}_{G}}^{*}T^{\mathbb{C}}\mathfrak{U}^{+}_{G}\cong f_{\mathfrak{B}_{G}}^{*}T^{\mathbb{C}}\mathfrak{B}_{G}
, such that
\bar{\partial}_{E}\varphi=0
. More specifically, for each
x\in X
, the fiber
E_{x}
of the principal
K^{\mathbb{C}}
-bundle
E
is
(\mathrm{Stab}_{G}(f(x)))^{\mathbb{C}}
, and the Higgs field
\varphi(x)
is a
(1,0)
-form with values in
A_{\mathbb{C}}
, such that
\bar{\partial}_{E}\varphi=0
.

An analogous definition is obtained for the lower half-space model
\mathfrak{U}_{G}^{-}
as well.

10.2.
Higgs bundles for models associated to groups
A^{\times}
and
\mathrm{O}(A_{\mathbb{C}},\sigma_{\mathbb{C}})

The groups
A^{\times}
and
\mathrm{O}(A_{\mathbb{C}},\sigma_{\mathbb{C}})
can be both embedded as subgroups into
\mathrm{O}_{(1,1)}(A,\sigma)
, thus the
G
-Higgs bundle data in these cases are described as special cases of the
\mathrm{O}_{(1,1)}(A,\sigma)
-definitions given above. We next provide these descriptions only for the indefinite involutive operators and the projective space models, while the
definitions for the half-space models and the precompact model associated to the groups
A^{\times}
and
\mathrm{O}(A_{\mathbb{C}},\sigma_{\mathbb{C}})
are obtained in an exactly analogous way.

10.2.1.
Groups
A^{\times}
.

As we have seen in Section
4
, consider a diagonal copy
\hat{G}
of the group
A^{\times}
into
\mathrm{O}_{(1,1)}(A,\sigma)
and a diagonal copy
\hat{K}
of a maximal compact subgroup
K=\mathrm{O}(A,\sigma)
. We then obtain the following:

Definition 10.5
.

A
polystable
\hat{G}
-Higgs bundle in the indefinite involutive operators model

\mathfrak{C}_{\hat{G}}
that comes from a reductive representation
\rho\colon\pi_{1}(X)\to\hat{G}
consists of a holomorphic principal
\hat{K}^{\mathbb{C}}
-bundle
(E,\bar{\partial}_{E})
together with a
(1,0)
-form
\varphi
with values in
f_{\mathfrak{C}_{\hat{G}}}^{*}T^{\mathbb{C}}\mathfrak{C}_{\hat{G}}
, such that
\bar{\partial}_{E}\varphi=0
. More specifically, for each
x\in X
, the fiber
E_{x}
of the principal
\hat{K}^{\mathbb{C}}
-bundle
E
is
(\mathrm{Stab}_{\hat{G}}(J_{x}))^{\mathbb{C}}
, and the Higgs field
\varphi(x)
is a
(1,0)
-form with values in

\displaystyle T_{J_{x}}^{\mathbb{C}}\mathfrak{C}_{\hat{G}}=\left\{L^{\prime}\colon A_{\mathbb{C}}^{2}\to A_{\mathbb{C}}^{2}\,\middle|\,\begin{aligned} &\ L^{\prime}\text{ is }A_{\mathbb{C}}\text{-linear},\\
&\ L^{\prime}J_{x}+J_{x}L^{\prime}=0,\text{ and }\\
&\ \omega_{\mathbb{C}}(L^{\prime}(\cdot),\cdot)+\omega_{\mathbb{C}}(\cdot,L^{\prime}(\cdot))=0\end{aligned}\right\},

such that
\bar{\partial}_{E}\varphi=0
.

Similarly, we obtain the data in the projective space model as follows:

Definition 10.6
.

A
polystable
\hat{G}
-Higgs bundle in the projective space model

\mathfrak{P}_{\hat{G}}^{+}
that comes from a reductive representation
\rho\colon\pi_{1}(X)\to\hat{G}
consists of a holomorphic
\hat{K}^{\mathbb{C}}
-bundle
(E,\bar{\partial}_{E})
defined by
f_{\mathfrak{P}_{\hat{G}}^{+}}^{\mathbb{C}}
together with a
(1,0)
-form
\varphi
with values in
f_{\mathfrak{P}_{\hat{G}}^{+}}^{*}T^{\mathbb{C}}\mathfrak{P}_{\hat{G}}^{+}
, such that
\bar{\partial}_{E}\varphi=0
. More specifically, for each
x\in\tilde{X}
, the fiber over
x
is an
A_{\mathbb{C}}
-line
l_{x}^{\mathbb{C}}:=f_{\mathfrak{P}_{\hat{G}}^{+}}(x)^{\mathbb{C}}
in
A_{\mathbb{C}}^{2}
which is equipped with an orthogonal
A
-valued form
h_{\mathbb{C}}
, the Higgs field
\varphi(x)
is a
(1,0)
-form with values in

T_{l_{x}}^{\mathbb{C}}\mathfrak{P}_{\hat{G}}^{+}=\{Q\in\mathrm{Hom}(l_{x}^{\mathbb{C}},l_{x}^{\mathbb{C}})\mid\text{for all $x\in l_{x}^{\mathbb{C}}$, }h_{\mathbb{C}}(Q(x),x)\in A^{\sigma_{\mathbb{C}}}_{\mathbb{C}}\},

such that
\bar{\partial}_{E}\varphi=0
.

10.2.2.
Groups
\mathrm{O}(A_{\mathbb{C}},\sigma_{\mathbb{C}})

We have seen in Section
5
that the group
G:=\mathrm{O}(A_{\mathbb{C}},\sigma_{\mathbb{C}})
is contained in
A_{\mathbb{C}}^{\times}
, thus
G
is embedded into
\mathrm{O}_{(1,1)}(A,\bar{\sigma}_{\mathbb{C}})
as a subgroup
\hat{G}
of diagonal matrices. Let
\hat{K}
denote the corresponding diagonal copy of
K:=\mathrm{O}(A,\sigma)
in
\mathrm{O}_{(1,1)}(A,\bar{\sigma}_{\mathbb{C}})
. We have the following:

Definition 10.7
.

A
polystable
\hat{G}
-Higgs bundle in the indefinite involutive operators model

\mathfrak{C}_{\hat{G}}
that comes from a reductive representation
\rho\colon\pi_{1}(X)\to\hat{G}
consists of a holomorphic principal
\hat{K}^{\mathbb{C}}
-bundle
(E,\bar{\partial}_{E})
together with a
(1,0)
-form
\varphi
with values in
f_{\mathfrak{C}_{\hat{G}}}^{*}T^{\mathbb{C}}\mathfrak{C}_{\hat{G}}
, such that
\bar{\partial}_{E}\varphi=0
. More specifically, for each
x\in X
, the fiber
E_{x}
of the principal
\hat{K}^{\mathbb{C}}
-bundle
E
is
(\mathrm{Stab}_{\hat{G}}(J_{x}))^{\mathbb{C}}
, and the Higgs field
\varphi(x)
is a 1-form with values in

\displaystyle T^{\mathbb{C}}_{J_{x}}\mathfrak{C}_{\hat{G}}=\left\{L\colon A_{\mathbb{C}}^{2}\to A_{\mathbb{C}}^{2}\,\middle|\,\begin{aligned} &\ L\text{ is }A_{\mathbb{C}}\text{-linear},\\
&\ LJ_{x}^{\mathbb{C}}+J_{x}^{\mathbb{C}}L=0,\ \text{and}\\
&\ b_{\mathbb{C}}(L(\cdot),\cdot)+b_{\mathbb{C}}(\cdot,L(\cdot))=0\end{aligned}\right\},

such that
\bar{\partial}_{E}\varphi=0
.

Similarly, we obtain the data in the projective space model as follows:

Definition 10.8
.

A
polystable
\hat{G}
-Higgs bundle in the projective space model

\mathfrak{P}_{\hat{G}}^{+}
that comes from a reductive representation
\rho\colon\pi_{1}(X)\to\hat{G}
consists of a holomorphic
\hat{K}^{\mathbb{C}}
-bundle
(E,\bar{\partial}_{E})
defined by
f_{\mathfrak{P}_{\hat{G}}^{+}}^{\mathbb{C}}
together with a
(1,0)
-form
\varphi
with values in
f_{\mathfrak{P}_{\hat{G}}^{+}}^{*}T^{\mathbb{C}}\mathfrak{P}_{\hat{G}}^{+}
, such that
\bar{\partial}_{E}\varphi=0
. More specifically, for each
x\in\tilde{X}
, the fiber over
x
is an
A_{\mathbb{C}}
-line
l_{x}^{\mathbb{C}}:=f_{\mathfrak{P}_{\hat{G}}^{+}}(x)^{\mathbb{C}}
in
A_{\mathbb{C}}^{2}
, the Higgs field
\varphi(x)
is a
(1,0)
-form with values in

T_{l_{x}}^{\mathbb{C}}\mathfrak{P}_{\hat{G}}^{+}=\{Q\in\mathrm{Hom}(l_{x}^{\mathbb{C}},l_{x}^{\mathbb{C}})\mid\text{for all $x\in l_{x}^{\mathbb{C}}$, }h_{\mathbb{C}}(Q(x),x)\in A^{-\sigma_{\mathbb{C}}}_{\mathbb{C}}\},

such that
\bar{\partial}_{E}\varphi=0
.

10.3.
Higgs bundles for models associated to groups
\mathrm{Sp}_{2}(A,\sigma)

We consider similarly the Higgs bundle data for symplectic groups
\mathrm{Sp}_{2}(A,\sigma)
over a Hermitian noncommutative algebra
A
with an anti-involution
\sigma
.

10.3.1.
Complex structures model.

Recall from Section
6.1
that
\mathfrak{C}_{G}
denotes the complex structures model of
\mathcal{X}
, for
x\in X
, let
J_{x}:=f_{\mathfrak{C}_{G}}(x)\in\mathfrak{C}_{G}
be the corresponding complex structure. Then we have the identification
\mathrm{Stab}_{G}(J_{x})\cong K
(see Section
9.2
), which acts on the tangent space

\displaystyle T_{J_{x}}\mathfrak{C}_{G}=\left\{L\colon A^{2}\to A^{2}\,\middle|\,\begin{aligned} &\ L\text{ is }A\text{-linear},\\
&\ h_{L}\text{ is }\sigma\text{-symmetric, and}\\
&\ LJ_{x}+J_{x}L=0\end{aligned}\right\};

note that
J_{x}
extends
\mathbb{C}
-linearly to an
A_{\mathbb{C}}
-linear operator
J_{x}^{\mathbb{C}}
on
A_{\mathbb{C}}^{2}
. Therefore, we can give the following description of Higgs bundles in terms of the model
\mathfrak{C}_{G}
.

Definition 10.9
.

A
polystable
G
-Higgs bundle in the complex structure model

\mathfrak{C}_{G}
that comes from a reductive representation
\rho\colon\pi_{1}(X)\to G
consists of a holomorphic principal
K^{\mathbb{C}}
-bundle
(E,\bar{\partial}_{E})
together with a
(1,0)
-form
\varphi
with values in
f_{\mathfrak{C}_{G}}^{*}T^{\mathbb{C}}\mathfrak{C}_{G}
, such that
\bar{\partial}_{E}\varphi=0
. More specifically, for each
x\in X
, the fiber
E_{x}
of the principal
K^{\mathbb{C}}
-bundle
E
is
(\mathrm{Stab}_{G}(J_{x}))^{\mathbb{C}}
, and the Higgs field
\varphi(x)
is a
(1,0)
-form with values in

\displaystyle T^{\mathbb{C}}_{J_{x}}\mathfrak{C}_{G}=\left\{L\colon A_{\mathbb{C}}^{2}\to A_{\mathbb{C}}^{2}\,\middle|\,\begin{aligned} &\ L\text{ is }A_{\mathbb{C}}\text{-linear},\\
&\ h_{L}\text{ is }\sigma_{\mathbb{C}}\text{-symmetric, and}\\
&\ LJ_{x}^{\mathbb{C}}+J_{x}^{\mathbb{C}}L=0\end{aligned}\right\},

such that
\bar{\partial}_{E}\varphi=0
.

10.3.2.
Projective space model.

Notice that, as we have already seen in Lemma
6.5
, any
L\in T^{\mathbb{C}}_{J}\mathfrak{C}_{G}
has a compatible structure of eigen-directions with
J_{\mathbb{C}}
. Namely, let
v_{\pm}\in A_{\mathbb{C}}^{2}
be such that
J_{\mathbb{C}}v_{\pm}=\pm iv_{\pm}
. Since
J
maps
A^{2}
to
A^{2}
,
v_{\pm}
can be chosen so that
v_{\pm}=\bar{v}_{\mp}
and, moreover,
Lv_{\pm}=v_{\mp}a_{\pm}
where
a_{\pm}\in A_{\mathbb{C}}^{\sigma_{\mathbb{C}}}
. This gives rise to two linear maps
L_{\pm}\colon l_{\pm}\to A_{\mathbb{C}}^{2}/l_{\pm}
mapping
v_{\pm}\mapsto Lv_{\pm}+l_{\pm}
where
l_{\pm}=v_{\pm}A_{\mathbb{C}}=\bar{v}_{\mp}A_{\mathbb{C}}=\bar{l}_{\mp}
. Notice that
L_{\pm}
represent tangent vectors of
\mathfrak{P}_{G}^{+}
at
l_{\pm}
. In this regard, we can identify the complexified tangent bundle
T^{\mathbb{C}}\mathfrak{P}_{G}^{+}
with the space of triples (cf. (
6.18
))

\{(l,L_{+},L_{-})\mid l\in\mathfrak{P}_{G}^{+},\;L_{+}\in T_{l}\mathfrak{P}_{G}^{+},\;L_{-}\in T_{\bar{l}}\mathfrak{P}_{G}^{-}\}.

As above, for
x\in X
we denote
l_{x}:=f_{\mathfrak{P}_{G}^{+}}(x)\in\mathfrak{P}_{G}^{+}
.

Definition 10.10
.

A
polystable
G
-Higgs bundle in the projective space model

\mathfrak{P}_{G}^{+}
that comes from a reductive representation
\rho\colon\pi_{1}(X)\to G
consists of a holomorphic principal
K^{\mathbb{C}}
-bundle
(E,\bar{\partial}_{E})
together with a
(1,0)
-form
\varphi
with values in
f_{\mathfrak{P}_{G}^{+}}^{*}T^{\mathbb{C}}\mathfrak{P}_{G}^{+}
, such that
\bar{\partial}_{E}\varphi=0
. More specifically, for each
x\in X
, the fiber
E_{x}
of the principal
K^{\mathbb{C}}
-bundle
E
is
(\mathrm{Stab}_{G}(l_{x}))^{\mathbb{C}}
, and the Higgs field
\varphi(x)
is a
(1,0)
-form with values in

\displaystyle T_{l_{x}}^{\mathbb{C}}\mathfrak{P}_{G}^{+}=\left\{L_{+}\colon l_{x}\to A^{2}_{\mathbb{C}}/l_{x},\;L_{-}\colon\bar{l}_{x}\to A^{2}_{\mathbb{C}}/\bar{l}_{x}\,\middle|\,\begin{aligned} &\ L_{\pm}\text{ are }A_{\mathbb{C}}\text{-linear},\\
&\ \omega(L_{\pm}(x),x)\in A^{\sigma_{\mathbb{C}}}_{\mathbb{C}}\text{ for all }x\in A_{\mathbb{C}}^{\sigma_{\mathbb{C}}}\end{aligned}\right\}

such that
\bar{\partial}_{E}\varphi=0
.

Similarly to Definition
10.3
and the discussion before it, we can reinterpret the principal
K^{\mathbb{C}}
-bundle and give an equivalent definition of a Higgs bundle in terms of the projective space model.

Definition 10.11
.

A
polystable
G
-Higgs bundle in the projective space model

\mathfrak{P}_{G}^{+}
that comes from a reductive representation
\rho\colon\pi_{1}(X)\to G
consists of a holomorphic
K^{\mathbb{C}}
-bundle
(E,\bar{\partial}_{E})
defined by
f_{\mathfrak{P}_{G}^{+}}
together with a
(1,0)
-form
\varphi
with values in
f_{\mathfrak{P}_{G}^{+}}^{*}T^{\mathbb{C}}\mathfrak{P}_{G}^{+}
, such that
\bar{\partial}_{E}\varphi=0
. More specifically, for each
x\in\tilde{X}
, the fiber over
x
consists of two
A_{\mathbb{C}}
-lines
l_{x}:=f_{\mathfrak{P}_{G}^{+}}(x)
and
\bar{l}_{x}
in
A_{\mathbb{C}}^{2}
, the Higgs field
\varphi(x)
is a
(1,0)
-form with values in

\displaystyle T_{l_{x}}^{\mathbb{C}}\mathfrak{P}_{G}^{+}=\left\{L_{+}\colon l_{x}\to A^{2}_{\mathbb{C}}/l_{x},\;L_{-}\colon\bar{l}_{x}\to A^{2}_{\mathbb{C}}/\bar{l}_{x}\,\middle|\,\begin{aligned} &\ L_{\pm}\text{ are }A_{\mathbb{C}}\text{-linear},\\
&\ \omega(L_{\pm}(x),x)\in A^{\sigma_{\mathbb{C}}}_{\mathbb{C}}\text{ for all }x\in A_{\mathbb{C}}^{\sigma_{\mathbb{C}}}\end{aligned}\right\},

such that
\bar{\partial}_{E}\varphi=0
.

Equivalently, we can say that the fiber over
x
of a Higgs bundle consists of two
A_{\mathbb{C}}
-lines
l_{x}
and
\bar{l}_{x}
, and a Higgs field which is a pair of
(1,0)
-form
(\varphi_{1},\varphi_{2})
with values in
A_{\mathbb{C}}
-homomorphism spaces:

\{L\colon l_{x}\to\bar{l}_{x}\mid\text{$L$ is $A_{\mathbb{C}}$-linear, $\omega(L(x),x)\in A^{\sigma_{\mathbb{C}}}_{\mathbb{C}}$ for all $x\in A_{\mathbb{C}}^{\sigma_{\mathbb{C}}}$}\},

resp.

\{L^{\prime}\colon\bar{l}_{x}\to l_{x}\mid\text{$L^{\prime}$ is $A_{\mathbb{C}}$-linear, $\omega(L^{\prime}(x),x)\in A^{\sigma_{\mathbb{C}}}_{\mathbb{C}}$ for all $x\in A_{\mathbb{C}}^{\sigma_{\mathbb{C}}}$}\}

such that
\bar{\partial}_{E}\varphi_{i}=0
.

10.3.3.
Upper half-space model and precompact model.

Since the upper half-space model
\mathfrak{U}_{G}^{+}
and the precompact model
\mathfrak{B}_{G}
are open domains in
A_{\mathbb{C}}^{\sigma_{\mathbb{C}}}
, the complexification of the tangent space for every point of
\mathfrak{U}_{G}^{+}
is naturally isomorphic to
A_{\mathbb{C}}^{\sigma_{\mathbb{C}}}\times A_{\mathbb{C}}^{\sigma_{\mathbb{C}}}
. Therefore,
f^{*}_{\mathfrak{U}_{G}^{+}}T^{\mathbb{C}}\mathfrak{U}_{G}^{+}
and
f^{*}_{\mathfrak{B}_{G}}T^{\mathbb{C}}\mathfrak{B}_{G}
identify canonically with
\tilde{X}\times(A_{\mathbb{C}}^{\sigma_{\mathbb{C}}})^{2}
and a Higgs field
\varphi(x)
is in both cases a holomorphic 1-form with values in
A_{\mathbb{C}}^{\sigma_{\mathbb{C}}}\times A_{\mathbb{C}}^{\sigma_{\mathbb{C}}}
.

The only difference between fibers of
\tilde{X}\times(A^{\sigma_{\mathbb{C}}}_{\mathbb{C}})^{2}
over different points
x\in\tilde{X}
is the action of the stabilizer
\mathrm{Stab}_{\mathrm{Sp}_{2}(A,\sigma)}(f(x))
, which is described in Section
6.3
(for the upper half-space model) and Section
6.4
(for the precompact model). We thus include here for completeness:

Definition 10.12
.

A
polystable
G
-Higgs bundle in the upper half-space model

\mathfrak{U}_{G}^{+}
or in the
precompact model

\mathfrak{B}_{G}
that comes from a reductive representation
\rho\colon\pi_{1}(X)\to G
consists of a holomorphic principal
K^{\mathbb{C}}
-bundle
(E,\bar{\partial}_{E})
together with a
(1,0)
-form
\varphi
with values in
f_{\mathfrak{U}_{G}^{+}}^{*}T^{\mathbb{C}}\mathfrak{U}_{G}^{+}\cong f_{\mathfrak{B}_{G}}^{*}T^{\mathbb{C}}\mathfrak{B}_{G}
, such that
\bar{\partial}_{E}\varphi=0
. More specifically, for each
x\in X
, the fiber
E_{x}
of the principal
K^{\mathbb{C}}
-bundle
E
is
(\mathrm{Stab}_{G}(f(x)))^{\mathbb{C}}
, and the Higgs field
\varphi(x)
is a
(1,0)
-form with values in
A_{\mathbb{C}}^{\sigma_{\mathbb{C}}}\times A_{\mathbb{C}}^{\sigma_{\mathbb{C}}}
, such that
\bar{\partial}_{E}\varphi=0
.

10.4.
Higgs bundles for models associated to groups
\mathrm{Sp}_{2}(A_{\mathbb{C}},\sigma_{\mathbb{C}})

We give next the description of Higgs bundle data for the quaternionic structures model of the symmetric space of groups
\mathrm{Sp}_{2}(A_{\mathbb{C}},\sigma_{\mathbb{C}})
which is the most interesting case; descriptions for the rest three models given in Section
7
can be obtained analogously.

Quaternionic structures model.

From the description of the complexified tangent space of the quaternionic structures model in (
7.4
), a Higgs field
\varphi(x)
is a holomorphic 1-form with values in

\displaystyle T^{\mathbb{C}}_{J_{x}}\mathfrak{C}_{G}=\left\{L\colon(A_{\mathbb{C}}\times A_{\mathbb{C}})^{2}\to(A_{\mathbb{C}}\times A_{\mathbb{C}})^{2}\,\middle|\,\begin{aligned} &\ L(xa)=L(x)\theta_{(i,i)}(a),\\
&\text{for }x\in(A_{\mathbb{C}}\times A_{\mathbb{C}})^{2}\text{ and }a\in A_{\mathbb{C}}\times A_{\mathbb{C}},\\
&\ h_{L}\text{ is }\sigma_{1}\text{-symmetric},\text{ and }\\
&\ LJ_{x}+J_{x}L=0\end{aligned}\right\}.

We thus give the definition of a polystable
\mathrm{Sp}_{2}(A_{\mathbb{C}},\sigma_{\mathbb{C}})
-Higgs bundle in this model:

Definition 10.13
.

A
polystable
G
-Higgs bundle in the quaternionic structures model

\mathfrak{C}_{G}
that comes from a reductive representation
\rho\colon\pi_{1}(X)\to G
, consists of a holomorphic principal
K^{\mathbb{C}}
-bundle
(E,\bar{\partial}_{E})
together with a
(1,0)
-form
\varphi
with values in
f_{\mathfrak{C}_{G}}^{*}T^{\mathbb{C}}\mathfrak{C}_{G}
, such that
\bar{\partial}_{E}\varphi=0
. More specifically, for each
x\in X
, the fiber
E_{x}
of the principal
K^{\mathbb{C}}
-bundle
E
is
(\mathrm{Stab}_{G}(f(x)))^{\mathbb{C}}
, and the Higgs field
\varphi(x)
is a 1-form with values in the space
T^{\mathbb{C}}_{J_{x}}\mathfrak{C}_{G}
described above and
\bar{\partial}_{E}\varphi=0
.

Projective space model.

In terms of the projective model, the definition of an
\mathrm{Sp}_{2}(A_{\mathbb{C}},\sigma_{\mathbb{C}})
-Higgs bundle can be given as follows:

Definition 10.14
.

A
polystable
G
-Higgs bundle in the projective space model

\mathfrak{P}_{G}^{+}
that comes from a reductive representation
\rho\colon\pi_{1}(X)\to G
consists of a holomorphic
K^{\mathbb{C}}
-bundle
(E,\bar{\partial}_{E})
defined by
f_{\mathfrak{P}_{G}^{+}}^{\mathbb{C}}
together with a
(1,0)
-form
\varphi
with values in
f_{\mathfrak{P}_{G}^{+}}^{*}T^{\mathbb{C}}\mathfrak{P}_{G}^{+}
, such that
\bar{\partial}_{E}\varphi=0
. More specifically, for each
x\in\tilde{X}
, the fiber over
x
is an
A_{\mathbb{H}}\otimes_{\mathbb{R}}\mathbb{C}
-line
l_{x}^{\mathbb{C}}:=f_{\mathfrak{P}_{G}^{+}}(x)^{\mathbb{C}}
in
A_{\mathbb{H}}^{2}\otimes_{\mathbb{R}}\mathbb{C}
which can be seen as a rank 2 free
A_{\mathbb{C}}
-module
\hat{l}_{x}
with a
\sigma_{\mathbb{C}}
-symplectic form
\hat{\omega}
(cf. (
7.10
) and the discussion before), the Higgs field
\varphi(x)
is a
(1,0)
-form with values in

T_{l_{x}}^{\mathbb{C}}\mathfrak{P}_{G}^{+}=\{Q\in\mathrm{Hom}(\hat{l}_{x},\hat{l}_{x})\mid\text{for all $x\in\hat{l}_{x}$, }\hat{\omega}(Q(x),x)+\hat{\omega}(x,Q(x))=0\},

such that
\bar{\partial}_{E}\varphi=0
.

11.
Examples

Following the general definitions for
G
-Higgs bundles given in Section
10
, we now illustrate examples of
G
-Higgs bundles in a number of different cases. Whenever applicable, we show explicitly that the definitions we get in these new models of the symmetric space are equivalent to classical definitions in the literature.

11.1.
Higgs bundles for
G=\mathrm{Sp}_{2n}(\mathbb{R})
and
G=\mathrm{Sp}_{2n}(\mathbb{C})

The model of the symmetric space we choose to work with in order to exhibit the equivalence of our definitions to the classical definitions of Higgs bundles for these groups is the complex structures model for the case
\mathrm{Sp}_{2n}(\mathbb{R})
and the quaternionic structures model for
\mathrm{Sp}_{2n}(\mathbb{C})
, because the group action on these models can be seen more clearly. The diffeomorphisms between the models from Sections
6
and
7
then allow one to see the appropriate identifications of data explicitly for any other model as well.

We begin with a revision of the classical definitions of
\mathrm{Sp}_{2n}(\mathbb{R})
-Higgs bundles and
\mathrm{Sp}_{2n}(\mathbb{C})
-Higgs bundles from
[
11
]
; we then show in Section
11.2
how the data from Definitions
10.9
and
10.13
that we introduced using the complex structures model and the quaternionic structures model respectively, can be explicitly related to these classical ones.

11.1.1.
G=\mathrm{Sp}_{2n}(\mathbb{R})

Let
X
be a compact Riemann surface with its canonical line bundle denoted as
K_{X}
, and let
G
be a real reductive group. The latter involves considering
Cartan data

\left(G,H,\theta,B\right)
, where
H\subset G
is a maximal compact subgroup,
\theta:\mathfrak{g}\to\mathfrak{g}
is a Cartan involution and
B
is a non-degenerate bilinear form on
\mathfrak{g}
, which is
\mathrm{Ad}(G)
-invariant and
\theta
-invariant. Moreover, the data
\left(G,H,\theta,B\right)
have to satisfy the following:

(1)

The Lie algebra
\mathfrak{g}
of the group
G
is reductive,

(2)

\theta
gives a decomposition (called the
Cartan decomposition
)

\mathfrak{g}=\mathfrak{h}\oplus\mathfrak{m}

into its
\pm 1
-eigenspaces, where
\mathfrak{h}
is the Lie algebra of
H
,

(3)

\mathfrak{h}
and
\mathfrak{m}
are orthogonal under
B
and
B
is positive definite on
\mathfrak{m}
and negative definite on
\mathfrak{h}
,

(4)

multiplication as a map from
H\times\exp\mathfrak{m}
into
G
is a diffeomorphism.

Let
{{H}^{\mathbb{C}}}
be the complexification of
H
and let
{{\mathfrak{g}}^{\mathbb{C}}}={{\mathfrak{h}}^{\mathbb{C}}}\oplus{{\mathfrak{m}}^{\mathbb{C}}}
be the complexification of the Cartan decomposition. The adjoint action of
G
on
\mathfrak{g}
restricts to give a representation (the
isotropy representation
) of
H
on
\mathfrak{m}
. This is independent of the choice of Cartan decomposition, since any two Cartan decompositions of
G
are related by a conjugation, using also that
\left[\mathfrak{h},\mathfrak{m}\right]\subseteq\mathfrak{m}
, and the same is true for the complexified isotropy representation

\iota\colon H^{\mathbb{C}}\to\mathrm{GL}\left(\mathfrak{m}^{\mathbb{C}}\right).

This introduces the following definition:

Definition 11.1
.

A
G
-Higgs bundle
over
X
is a pair
\left(E,\varphi\right)
where

•

E
is a principal holomorphic
{{H}^{\mathbb{C}}}
-bundle over
X
and

•

\varphi
is a holomorphic section of the vector bundle
E\left({{\mathfrak{m}}^{\mathbb{C}}}\right)\otimes K_{X}=\left(E{{\times}_{\iota}}{{\mathfrak{m}}^{\mathbb{C}}}\right)\otimes K_{X}
.

The section
\varphi
is called the
Higgs field
.

Let us consider now the special case when the structure group is
G=\mathrm{Sp}_{2n}(\mathbb{R})
in particular.
More specifically, the group
G=\mathrm{Sp}_{2n}(\mathbb{R})
is the semisimple real subgroup of
\mathrm{SL}_{2n}(\mathbb{R})
that preserves a symplectic form on
{{\mathbb{R}}^{2n}}
:

\mathrm{Sp}_{2n}(\mathbb{R})=\left\{A\in\mathrm{SL}_{2n}(\mathbb{R})\left|{{A}^{t}}{\omega}A={\omega}\right.\right\},

where
{\omega}=\left(\begin{matrix}0&{{I}_{n}}\\
-{{I}_{n}}&0\\
\end{matrix}\right)
defines a symplectic form on
{{\mathbb{R}}^{2n}}
, for
{{I}_{n}}
the (
n\times n
)-identity matrix. The complexification of its Lie algebra

{{\mathfrak{g}}^{\mathbb{C}}}=\mathfrak{sp}_{2n}\left(\mathbb{C}\right)=\left\{\left(\begin{matrix}A&B\\
C&-{{A}^{t}}\\
\end{matrix}\right)\,\middle|\,A,B,C\in{{\mathsf{\mathrm{Mat}}}_{n}}\left(\mathbb{C}\right);{{B}^{t}}=B,{{C}^{t}}=C\right\}

has split real form
\mathfrak{sp}_{2n}\left(\mathbb{R}\right)
and compact real form
\mathfrak{sp}(n)
.

The Cartan involution
\theta:\mathfrak{sp}_{2n}\left(\mathbb{C}\right)\to\mathfrak{sp}_{2n}\left(\mathbb{C}\right)
with
\theta\left(X\right)=-{{X}^{t}}
determines a Cartan decomposition for a choice of maximal compact subgroup
H\cong\mathrm{U}(n)\subset\mathrm{Sp}_{2n}(\mathbb{R})
as

\mathfrak{sp}_{2n}\left(\mathbb{R}\right)=\mathfrak{u}\left(n\right)\oplus\mathfrak{m}

with complexification

(11.2)

\mathfrak{sp}_{2n}\left(\mathbb{C}\right)=\mathfrak{gl}_{n}\left(\mathbb{C}\right)\oplus{{\mathfrak{m}}^{\mathbb{C}}}.

Applying the change of basis on
{{\mathbb{C}}^{2n}}
effected by the mapping
T=\left(\begin{matrix}I_{n}&iI_{n}\\
I_{n}&-iI_{n}\\
\end{matrix}\right)
, we can identify the summands in the Cartan decomposition of
\mathfrak{sp}_{2n}\left(\mathbb{C}\right)\subset\mathfrak{sl}_{2n}\left(\mathbb{C}\right)
as:

\displaystyle\mathfrak{gl}_{n}\left(\mathbb{C}\right)

\displaystyle=\left\{\left(\begin{matrix}Z&0\\
0&-{{Z}^{t}}\\
\end{matrix}\right)\,\middle|\,Z\in{{\mathsf{\mathrm{Mat}}}_{n}}\left(\mathbb{C}\right)\right\},

\displaystyle{{\mathfrak{m}}^{\mathbb{C}}}

\displaystyle=\left\{\left(\begin{matrix}0&\beta\\
\gamma&0\\
\end{matrix}\right)\,\middle|\,\,\beta,\gamma\in{{\mathsf{\mathrm{Mat}}}_{n}}\left(\mathbb{C}\right);{{\beta}^{t}}=\beta,{{\gamma}^{t}}=\gamma\right\}.

Note that the Cartan decomposition (
11.2
) depends on the choice of symplectic form
\omega
. The Cartan decomposition and the choice of basis on
\mathbb{C}^{2n}
by
T
above, induce a splitting of
\mathbb{C}^{2n}
in two copies of
\mathbb{C}^{n}
. Then, the symplectic form
\omega
provides an identification of one copy
\mathbb{C}^{n}
with the dual vector space
(\mathbb{C}^{n})^{*}
of the second copy. Therefore, a pair
(\beta,\gamma)
in
\mathfrak{m}^{\mathbb{C}}
as above can be rather seen as a pair belonging in the space

\mathrm{Sym}^{2}\left({{\mathbb{C}}^{n}}\right)\oplus\mathrm{Sym}^{2}\left({{\left({{\mathbb{C}}^{n}}\right)}^{*}}\right).

Let
V
denote the rank
n
vector bundle associated to a holomorphic principal
\mathrm{GL}_{n}(\mathbb{C})
-bundle
E
via the standard representation. Then from the Cartan decomposition for the Lie algebra
\mathfrak{sp}_{2n}\left(\mathbb{C}\right)
we can identify

E\left({{\mathfrak{m}}^{\mathbb{C}}}\right)={\mathrm{Sym}}^{2}\left(V\right)\oplus{\mathrm{Sym}}^{2}\left({{V}^{*}}\right)

and so the general definition for a
G
-Higgs bundle specializes to the following:

Definition 11.3
.

An
\mathrm{Sp}_{2n}(\mathbb{R})
-Higgs bundle over
X
is defined by a triple
\left(V,\beta,\gamma\right)
, where
V
is a rank
n
holomorphic vector bundle over
X
and
\beta,\gamma
are symmetric homomorphisms

\beta:{{V}^{*}}\to V\otimes K_{X}\text{ and }\gamma:V\to{{V}^{*}}\otimes K_{X}.

The embedding
\mathrm{Sp}_{2n}(\mathbb{R})\hookrightarrow\mathrm{SL}_{2n}(\mathbb{C})
allows one to reinterpret the defining
\mathrm{Sp}_{2n}(\mathbb{R})
-data of a Higgs bundle as special
\mathrm{SL}_{2n}(\mathbb{C})
-data. We can thus consider an
\mathrm{Sp}_{2n}(\mathbb{R})
-Higgs bundle to be defined as a pair
\left(E,\Phi\right)
, where

(1)

E=V\oplus{{V}^{*}}
is a rank
2n
holomorphic vector bundle over
X
and

(2)

\Phi:E\to E\otimes K_{X}
is a Higgs field with
\Phi=\left(\begin{matrix}0&\beta\\
\gamma&0\\
\end{matrix}\right)
,

for
V
,
\beta
,
\gamma
as above.

11.1.2.
G=\mathrm{Sp}_{2n}(\mathbb{C})

We now consider a complex reductive Lie group
G^{c}
. The general definition of a Higgs bundle in this case is the following.

Definition 11.4
.

A
G^{c}
-Higgs bundle over
X
is a pair
(P,\Phi)
where
P
is a holomorphic principal
G^{c}
-bundle over
X
and
\Phi
is a holomorphic section of the vector bundle
\mathrm{ad}P\otimes_{\mathbb{C}}K_{X}
, for
\mathrm{ad}P
the vector bundle associated to
P
via the adjoint representation.

In the special case when
G^{c}=\mathrm{Sp}_{2n}(\mathbb{C})
, the above definition specializes to the following:

Definition 11.5
.

An
\mathrm{Sp}_{2n}(\mathbb{C})
-Higgs bundle over
X
is a pair
(E,\Phi)
where
E
is a rank
2n
holomorphic vector bundle with a symplectic form
\omega(\cdot,\cdot)
, and
\Phi
is a holomorphic section in
H^{0}(X,\mathrm{End}(E)\otimes K_{X})
satisfying

\omega(\Phi v,w)=-\omega(v,\Phi w).

11.2.
Comparison of definitions

We see next that, when restricting to the case
\mathrm{Sp}_{2}(A,\sigma)
or
\mathrm{Sp}_{2}(A_{\mathbb{C}},\sigma_{\mathbb{C}})
, the definitions introduced above agree with the classical definitions.

11.2.1.
G=\mathrm{Sp}_{2n}(\mathbb{R})

From (
6.9
), the complexified tangent bundle of the space of all complex structures at a point
J\in\mathfrak{C}_{G}
is given by

\displaystyle T^{\mathbb{C}}_{J}\mathfrak{C}_{G}

\displaystyle=\left\{L_{\mathbb{C}}\colon A_{\mathbb{C}}^{2}\to A_{\mathbb{C}}^{2}\,\middle|\,\begin{aligned} &\ L_{\mathbb{C}}\text{ is $\mathbb{C}$-linear},\\
&\ L_{\mathbb{C}}J_{\mathbb{C}}+J_{\mathbb{C}}L_{\mathbb{C}}=0,\text{ and}\\
&\ h_{L_{\mathbb{C}}}\text{ is $\sigma_{\mathbb{C}}$-symmetric}\end{aligned}\right\}

\displaystyle\cong\{(a_{+},a_{-})\in A_{\mathbb{C}}^{\sigma_{\mathbb{C}}}\times A_{\mathbb{C}}^{\sigma_{\mathbb{C}}}\},

and this description depends on the choice of a basis
v=(v_{+},v_{-})
of
i
- and
-i
-eigenlines
l_{+}
and
l_{-}
of
J_{\mathbb{C}}
such that
\omega_{\mathbb{C}}(v_{+},v_{-})=-i
and
v_{+}=\bar{v}_{-}
. Therefore, using the form
\omega_{\mathbb{C}}
, the isotropic line
l_{+}
becomes dual to
l_{-}
.

For the case
(A,\sigma)=(\mathrm{Mat}_{n}(\mathbb{R}),(\bullet)^{t})
when
\mathrm{Sp}_{2}(A,\sigma)\cong\mathrm{Sp}_{2n}(\mathbb{R})
, the anti-involution
{{\sigma}_{\mathbb{C}}}
is defined as
{{\sigma}_{\mathbb{C}}}\left(a+bi\right)={{a}^{t}}+{b^{t}}i
, and we have

{A}_{\mathbb{C}}=\left\{a+bi\mid a,b\in A\right\}

and

\displaystyle A_{\mathbb{C}}^{{{\sigma}_{\mathbb{C}}}}=\left\{a+bi\mid a={{a}^{t}},b={{b}^{t}}\right\}=\mathrm{Sym}_{n}\left(\mathbb{R}\right)\oplus i\mathrm{Sym}_{n}(\mathbb{R})=\mathrm{Sym}_{n}(\mathbb{C}).

Moreover,
L_{\mathbb{C}}
in matrix presentation with respect to the basis
v
has the form
\begin{pmatrix}0&a_{-}\\
a_{+}&0\end{pmatrix}
,
for the morphisms
a_{-}\colon l_{-}\to l_{+}
and
a_{+}\colon l_{+}\to l_{-}
. The pullback bundle of
T^{\mathbb{C}}\mathfrak{C}_{G}
via a
\rho
-equivariant map
f
twisted by the canonical line bundle
K_{X}
has sections which are
identified with a morphism

E_{H^{\mathbb{C}}}(A^{2}_{\mathbb{C}})\to E_{H^{\mathbb{C}}}(A^{2}_{\mathbb{C}})\otimes K_{X},

where
E_{H^{\mathbb{C}}}(A^{2}_{\mathbb{C}})
denotes the associated bundle to the principal
H^{\mathbb{C}}
-bundle for the maximal compact subgroup
H\subset\mathrm{Sp}_{2}(A,\sigma)
. An endomorphism of
E_{H^{\mathbb{C}}}(A^{2}_{\mathbb{C}})
can be written in matrix presentation with respect to the basis
v
as
\left(\begin{matrix}0&a_{-}\\
a_{+}&0\\
\end{matrix}\right)
. In particular, under this 1-1 correspondence the isotropic line
l_{+}
corresponds to
V
from Definition
11.3
and
l_{-}
corresponds to
V^{*}
.

11.2.2.
G=\mathrm{Sp}_{2n}(\mathbb{C})

We now consider the definition of the Higgs bundle for the quaternionic structures model. Let
A=\mathrm{Mat}_{n}(\mathbb{R})
with
\sigma
being the transposition, then
A_{\mathbb{C}}=\mathrm{Mat}_{n}(\mathbb{C})
. A Higgs field at
x\in X
is a
1
-form with values in

\displaystyle T^{\mathbb{C}}_{J_{x}}\mathfrak{C}_{G}=\left\{L\colon A_{\mathbb{C}\mathbb{C}}^{2}\to A_{\mathbb{C}\mathbb{C}}^{2}\,\middle|\,\begin{aligned} &\ L(xa)=L(x)\theta_{(i,i)}(a)\;\text{ for }x\in A_{\mathbb{C}\mathbb{C}}^{2},a\in A_{\mathbb{C}\mathbb{C}},\\
&\ h_{L}\text{ is }\sigma_{1}\text{-symmetric},\text{ and }LJ_{x}+J_{x}L=0\end{aligned}\right\},

where
A_{\mathbb{C}\mathbb{C}}=A_{\mathbb{C}}\times A_{\mathbb{C}}
and
\sigma_{1}(z_{1},z_{2})=(\bar{\sigma}_{\mathbb{C}}(z_{2}),\bar{\sigma}_{\mathbb{C}}(z_{1}))
(cf. Section
7.1
). We choose a local frame in which
J_{x}=\Omega=\begin{pmatrix}0&\mathrm{Id}_{n}\\
-\mathrm{Id}_{n}&0\end{pmatrix}
and identify in the following the linear maps
J_{x}
and
L
with their matrices with respect to this frame.
The condition that
h_{L}
is
\sigma_{1}
-symmetric means that
\sigma_{1}(L)\Omega=\Omega L
. Let
L=\begin{pmatrix}a&b\\
c&d\end{pmatrix}
where
a=(a_{1},a_{2})
,
b=(b_{1},b_{2})
,
c=(c_{1},c_{2})
,
d=(d_{1},d_{2})
are elements of
A_{\mathbb{C}\mathbb{C}}
. This implies

\bar{\sigma}_{\mathbb{C}}(a_{2})=a_{1},\,\bar{\sigma}_{\mathbb{C}}(d_{2})=d_{1},\text{ and }

\bar{\sigma}_{\mathbb{C}}(c_{2})=b_{1},\,\bar{\sigma}_{\mathbb{C}}(b_{2})=c_{1}.

The condition
LJ_{x}+J_{x}L=0
additionally implies:

b_{1}=\bar{c}_{2},\,b_{2}=\bar{c}_{1},

a_{1}=-\bar{d}_{2},\,a_{2}=-\bar{d}_{1},\text{ and }

c_{1}=\sigma_{\mathbb{C}}(c_{1}),\,c_{2}=\sigma_{\mathbb{C}}(c_{2}).

Thus,
L=\begin{pmatrix}(a_{1},\bar{\sigma}_{\mathbb{C}}(a_{1}))&(\bar{c}_{2},\bar{c}_{1})\\
(c_{1},c_{2})&-(\sigma_{\mathbb{C}}(a_{1}),\bar{a}_{1})\end{pmatrix}
, where
a_{1}\in A_{\mathbb{C}}
,
c_{1},c_{2}\in A_{\mathbb{C}}^{\sigma_{\mathbb{C}}}
.

In the classical definition of the Higgs field as a
1
-from with values in
\mathfrak{m}^{\mathbb{C}}
, where
\mathfrak{m}
is the symmetric part in the Cartan decomposition

\mathfrak{sp}_{2n}(\mathbb{C})=\mathfrak{k}+\mathfrak{m},

\mathfrak{m}
is the space of Hermitian symplectic matrices:

\mathfrak{m}=\left\{\begin{pmatrix}a&b\\
\bar{b}&-\sigma(a)\end{pmatrix}\,\middle|\,a\in A_{\mathbb{C}}^{\bar{\sigma}_{\mathbb{C}}},\,b\in A_{\mathbb{C}}^{\sigma_{\mathbb{C}}}\right\}.

Moreover, the complexification of
A^{\bar{\sigma}_{\mathbb{C}}}_{\mathbb{C}}
is the space if
\sigma_{1}
-symmetric elements of
A_{\mathbb{C}\mathbb{C}}
, i.e. the space of all pairs
(a,\bar{\sigma}_{\mathbb{C}}(a))
where
a\in A_{\mathbb{C}}
. The complexification of
A^{\sigma_{\mathbb{C}}}_{\mathbb{C}}
is just its double
A^{\sigma_{\mathbb{C}}}_{\mathbb{C}}\times A^{\sigma_{\mathbb{C}}}_{\mathbb{C}}
, thus we obtain

\mathfrak{m}^{\mathbb{C}}=\left\{\begin{pmatrix}(a,\bar{\sigma}_{\mathbb{C}}(a))&(b_{1},b_{2})\\
(\bar{b}_{2},\bar{b}_{1})&-(\sigma_{\mathbb{C}}(a),\bar{a})\end{pmatrix}\,\middle|\,a\in A_{\mathbb{C}},\,b_{1},b_{2}\in A_{\mathbb{C}}^{\sigma_{\mathbb{C}}}\right\}.

Therefore the description of the Higgs field in the quaternionic structures model agrees with the classical description.

One can also see that an
\mathrm{Sp}_{2n}(\mathbb{C})
-Higgs bundle viewed as an
\mathrm{Sp}_{2}(A_{\mathbb{C}},\sigma_{\mathbb{C}})
-Higgs bundle is exactly the same as an
\mathrm{Sp}_{2n}(\mathbb{C})
-Higgs bundle in the classical sense, when considering the projective space model. Indeed, following the description of the complexified tangent bundle provided in (
7.10
), we obtain the following definition:

Definition 11.6
.

A
polystable
G=\mathrm{Sp}_{2n}(\mathbb{C})
-Higgs bundle in the projective space model

\mathfrak{P}_{G}^{+}
that comes from a reductive representation
\rho\colon\pi_{1}(X)\to G
consists of a holomorphic
K^{\mathbb{C}}
-bundle
(E,\bar{\partial}_{E})
defined by
f_{\mathfrak{P}_{G}^{+}}^{\mathbb{C}}
together with a
(1,0)
-form
\varphi
with values in
f_{\mathfrak{P}_{G}^{+}}^{*}T^{\mathbb{C}}\mathfrak{P}_{G}^{+}
, such that
\bar{\partial}_{E}\varphi=0
. More specifically, for each
x\in\tilde{X}
, the fiber over
x
is a
2n
-dimensional complex vector space
L_{x}^{\mathbb{C}}:=f_{\mathfrak{P}_{G}^{+}}(x)^{\mathbb{C}}
equipped with a symplectic form
\omega
, the Higgs field
\varphi(x)
is a
(1,0)
-form with values in

T_{L_{x}}^{\mathbb{C}}\mathfrak{P}_{G}^{+}=\{Q\in\mathrm{Hom}(L_{x}^{\mathbb{C}},L_{x}^{\mathbb{C}})\mid\text{for all $x\in L_{x}^{\mathbb{C}}$, }\omega(Q(x),x)+\omega(x,Q(x))=0\},

such that
\bar{\partial}_{E}\varphi=0
.

11.3.
More examples of Higgs bundles

11.3.1.
G=\mathrm{O}(n,n)

The maximal compact subgroup of
G=\mathrm{O}(n,n)
is
K:=\mathrm{O}(n)\times\mathrm{O}(n)
. The complexified tangent space in terms of the half-space model or the precompact model is naturally isomorphic to
\mathrm{Mat}_{n}(\mathbb{C})
.

Definition 11.7
.

An
\mathrm{O}(n,n)
-Higgs bundle
over
X
associated to a reductive representation
\rho\colon\pi_{1}(X)\to G
with a
\rho
-equivariant harmonic map
f\colon\tilde{X}\to\mathfrak{B}_{G}\cong G/K
is a triple
(V_{1},V_{2},\varphi)
, where
V_{1},V_{2}
are holomorphic principal
\mathrm{O}_{n}(\mathbb{C})
-bundles over
X
and
\varphi
is a holomorphic equivariant section of the pull-back bundle
f^{*}T^{\mathbb{C}}\mathfrak{B}_{G}\otimes K_{X}
which is a holomorphic bundle over
\tilde{X}
with fibers isomorphic to
\mathrm{Mat}_{n}(\mathbb{C})
.

We can also give a definition of an
\mathrm{O}(n,n)
-Higgs bundle in terms of the projective space model:

Definition 11.8
.

An
\mathrm{O}(n,n)
-Higgs bundle
over
X
associated to a reductive representation
\rho\colon\pi_{1}(X)\to G
with a
\rho
-equivariant harmonic map
f\colon\tilde{X}\to\mathfrak{P}_{G}^{+}\cong G/K
is a triple
(V_{1},V_{2},\varphi)
, where
V_{1},V_{2}
are holomorphic principal
\mathrm{O}_{n}(\mathbb{C})
-bundles over
X
. This can be interpreted as two holomorphic rank
n
vector bundles equipped with non-degenerate orthogonal forms
\omega_{1}
,
\omega_{2}
. The Higgs field in terms of the projective space model is a holomorphic equivariant section of the pull-back bundle
f^{*}T^{\mathbb{C}}\mathfrak{P}_{G}^{+}\otimes K_{X}
which can be seen as a holomorphic morphism
\varphi\colon V_{1}\to V_{2}\otimes K_{X}
(cf. (
3.5
)).

Remark 11.9
.

Since the forms
\omega_{1}
and
\omega_{2}
are non-degenerate, there is a unique morphism
\varphi^{\prime}\colon V_{2}\to V_{1}\otimes K_{X}
defined as follows

(11.10)

\omega_{1}(v_{1},\varphi^{\prime}(v_{2}))=\omega_{2}(\varphi(v_{1}),v_{2}),

for
v_{1}\in V_{1}
,
v_{2}\in V_{2}
.
If we choose local orthonormal bases of
V_{1}
and
V_{2}
, then the matrices
\Phi
and
\Phi^{\prime}
corresponding to
\varphi
and
\varphi^{\prime}
in these bases are related as follows:
\Phi^{\prime}=\Phi^{t}
and
\Phi\in\mathrm{Mat}_{n}(\mathbb{C})
. This corresponds (fiberwise) to the element in
\mathrm{Mat}_{n}(\mathbb{C})
from Definition
11.7
.

The above description is equivalent to the definition of general
\mathrm{O}(n,n)
-Higgs bundles in the classical sense (see
[
2
, Section 2]
), here “general” means Higgs bundles not necessarily coming from reductive representations, and “in the classical sense” means coming from the complexification of the Cartan decomposition.

Definition 11.11
.

An
\mathrm{O}(n,n)
-Higgs bundle
over
X
is a tuple
(V_{1},\omega_{1},V_{2},\omega_{2},\varphi)
for which
(V_{1},\omega_{1})
and
(V_{2},\omega_{2})
are rank
n
holomorphic orthogonal bundles, and
\varphi:V_{1}\to V_{2}\otimes K_{X}
is a holomorphic morphism.

11.3.2.
G=\mathrm{GL}_{n}(\mathbb{R})
and
\mathrm{O}_{n}(\mathbb{C})

In terms of the precompact model, the definition of
G
-Higgs bundles in these cases looks very similar. Embedded into
\mathrm{O}(n,n)
, the groups have the same maximal compact subgroup
K=\mathrm{O}(n)
(embedded diagonally), so the principal
K^{\mathbb{C}}
-bundle for both groups is the same. The only difference appears in the Higgs field. In terms of precompact model, the complexified tangent space at every point of the symmetric space is naturally isomorphic either to
\mathrm{Sym}_{n}(\mathbb{C})
or
\mathrm{Skew}_{n}(\mathbb{C})
. We thus have the following:

Definition 11.12
.

A
\mathrm{GL}_{n}(\mathbb{R})
-Higgs bundle (resp.
\mathrm{O}_{n}(\mathbb{C})
-Higgs bundle)
over
X
associated to a reductive representation
\rho\colon\pi_{1}(X)\to G
with a
\rho
-equivariant harmonic map
f\colon\tilde{X}\to\mathfrak{B}_{G}\cong G/K
is a pair
(V,\varphi)
, where
V
is a holomorphic principal
\mathrm{O}_{n}(\mathbb{C})
-bundle over
X
and
\varphi
is a holomorphic equivariant section of the pull-back bundle
f^{*}T^{\mathbb{C}}\mathfrak{B}_{G}\otimes K_{X}
which is a holomorphic bundle over
\tilde{X}
with fibers isomorphic to
\mathrm{Sym}_{n}(\mathbb{C})
(resp.
\mathrm{Skew}_{n}(\mathbb{C})
).

This can be interpreted as a holomorphic rank
n
vector bundle equipped with a non-degenerate orthogonal from
\omega
, and
\varphi\colon V\to V\otimes K_{X}
is a holomorphic morphism which is symmetric (resp. anti-symmetric) with respect to
\omega
.

11.3.3.
G=\mathrm{U}(n,n)

We start by reviewing the definition of
\mathrm{U}(n,n)
-Higgs bundles in the classical sense (see
[
14
]
). The maximal compact subgroup of
G
is
K:=\mathrm{U}(n)\times\mathrm{U}(n)
, with complexification
K^{\mathbb{C}}=\mathrm{GL}_{n}(\mathbb{C})\times\mathrm{GL}_{n}(\mathbb{C})
. Note that the complexification of the Cartan decomposition of
G
is

\mathfrak{gl}_{2n}(\mathbb{C})=\Big(\mathfrak{gl}_{n}(\mathbb{C})\oplus\mathfrak{gl}_{n}(\mathbb{C})\Big)\oplus\mathfrak{m}^{\mathbb{C}}.

If we embed
\mathfrak{gl}_{n}(\mathbb{C})\oplus\mathfrak{gl}_{n}(\mathbb{C})
into
\mathfrak{gl}_{2n}(\mathbb{C})
as block diagonal matrices, then

\mathfrak{m}^{\mathbb{C}}=\bigg\{\begin{pmatrix}0&*\\
*&0\end{pmatrix}\in\mathfrak{gl}_{2n}(\mathbb{C})\bigg\},

i.e. it consists of the off-diagonal matrices. This leads to the following definition of general
\mathrm{U}(n,n)
-Higgs bundles in the classical sense:

Definition 11.13
.

A
\mathrm{U}(n,n)
-Higgs bundle
consists of a quadruple
(V_{1},V_{2},\beta,\gamma)
so that
V_{1}
and
V_{2}
are rank
n
holomorphic vector bundles and
\beta:V_{2}\to V_{1}\otimes K_{X}
,
\gamma:V_{1}\to V_{2}\otimes K_{X}
are holomorphic morphisms.

Note that
(V_{1},V_{2},\beta,\gamma)
embeds as a
\mathrm{GL}_{2n}(\mathbb{C})
-Higgs bundle
(E=V_{1}\oplus V_{2},\varphi=\begin{pmatrix}0&\beta\\
\gamma&0\end{pmatrix})
.

Now we define
\mathrm{U}(n,n)
-Higgs bundles in the sense of
\mathrm{Sp}_{2}(A,\bar{\sigma})
and
\mathrm{O}_{(1,1)}(A,\bar{\sigma})
, where
A=\mathrm{Mat}_{n}(\mathbb{C})
and
\bar{\sigma}(X):=\bar{X}^{t}
. Remember from (
2.42
) that
\mathrm{Sp}_{2}(A,\bar{\sigma})\cong\mathrm{O}_{(1,1)}(A,\bar{\sigma})\cong\mathrm{U}(n,n)
) with a maximal compact subgroup
\mathrm{O}(A,\bar{\sigma})\times\mathrm{O}(A,\bar{\sigma})\cong\mathrm{U}(n)\times\mathrm{U}(n)
. The complexification of this group is isomorphic to
\mathrm{GL}_{n}(\mathbb{C})\times\mathrm{GL}_{n}(\mathbb{C})
. However, to provide a description of a
\mathrm{U}(n,n)
-Higgs bundle in terms of models, we need to understand this isomorphism more precisely. The complexification
A\otimes_{\mathbb{R}}\mathbb{C}\{I\}
of
A
can be identified with
A\times A
using the isomorphism from
[
1
, Appendix B.1.1]
mapping
a_{0}+a_{1}I
to
(a_{0}+a_{1}i,a_{0}-a_{1}i)
. Under this identification the elements of
a\in A\otimes_{\mathbb{R}}\mathbb{C}\{I\}
such that
\bar{\sigma}_{\mathbb{C}}(a)a=1
where
\bar{\sigma}_{\mathbb{C}}
is the
\mathbb{C}\{I\}
-linear extension of
\bar{\sigma}
are mapped to pairs
(x,\bar{\sigma}(x)^{-1})
, i.e. the image of
\mathrm{O}(A\otimes_{\mathbb{R}}\mathbb{C}\{I\},\bar{\sigma}_{\mathbb{C}})
is
\{(x,\bar{\sigma}(x)^{-1})\mid x\in A^{\times}\}
. The projection to the first component gives a group isomorphism between
\mathrm{O}(A\otimes_{\mathbb{R}}\mathbb{C}\{I\},\bar{\sigma}_{\mathbb{C}})
and
A^{\times}
.

Regarding
(A,\bar{\sigma})
as a real Hermitian algebra, then for the group
G=\mathrm{O}_{(1,1)}(A,\bar{\sigma})
, from Section
3
it follows that the complexified tangent space in terms of the half-space or the precompact model is naturally identified with
A_{\mathbb{C}}=\mathrm{Mat}_{n}(\mathbb{C})\otimes\mathbb{C}\{I\}
which is isomorphic to
\mathrm{Mat}_{n}(\mathbb{C})\times\mathrm{Mat}_{n}(\mathbb{C})
under the isomorphism described above. We thus have the following:

Definition 11.14
.

A
\mathrm{U}(n,n)
-Higgs bundle
over
X
associated to a reductive representation
\rho\colon\pi_{1}(X)\to G=\mathrm{O}_{(1,1)}(A,\bar{\sigma})
with a
\rho
-equivariant harmonic map
f\colon\tilde{X}\to\mathfrak{B}_{G}\cong G/K
is a holomorphic
\mathrm{GL}_{n}(\mathbb{C})\times\mathrm{GL}_{n}(\mathbb{C})
-principal bundle over
X
which can be interpreted as a pair of holomorphic
\mathrm{GL}_{n}(\mathbb{C})
-principal bundles over
X
and
\varphi
is a holomorphic equivariant section of the pull-back bundle
f^{*}T^{\mathbb{C}}\mathfrak{B}_{G}\otimes K_{X}
, which is a holomorphic bundle over
\tilde{X}
with fibers isomorphic to
\mathrm{Mat}_{n}(\mathbb{C})\times\mathrm{Mat}_{n}(\mathbb{C})
.

In order to give a definition of a
\mathrm{U}(n,n)
-Higgs bundle in terms of the projective space model for
\mathrm{O}_{(1,1)}(A,\bar{\sigma})
, we first notice that the principal
\mathrm{GL}_{n}(\mathbb{C})\times\mathrm{GL}_{n}(\mathbb{C})
-bundle can be interpreted as a bundle whose fiber is a pair of two
A_{\mathbb{C}}=A\otimes_{\mathbb{R}}\mathbb{C}
-lines
l_{1}^{\mathbb{C}}
and
l_{2}^{\mathbb{C}}
in
A_{\mathbb{C}}^{2}
such that
l_{i}^{\mathbb{C}}=l_{i}\otimes_{\mathbb{R}}\mathbb{C}
where
l_{i}
are
A
-lines in
(A^{2},\omega)
where
\omega
is the standard indefinite orthogonal form. Moreover,
l_{2}=l_{1}^{\perp_{\omega}}
and
\omega|_{l_{1}}
is positive definite. The Higgs field can be then interpreted as a morphism
\varphi\colon l_{1}^{\mathbb{C}}\to l_{2}^{\mathbb{C}}\otimes K_{X}
without any additional conditions. It also defines a unique morphism
\varphi^{\prime}\colon l_{2}^{\mathbb{C}}\to l_{1}^{\mathbb{C}}\otimes K_{X}
by the condition
\omega_{\mathbb{C}}(v_{1},\varphi^{\prime}(v_{2}))=\omega_{\mathbb{C}}(\varphi(v_{1}),v_{2})
. However, in this case
A_{\mathbb{C}}
is isomorphic to
A\times A
and the real locus
A\subset A_{\mathbb{C}}
maps to the diagonal
\{(a,a)\mid a\in A\}\in A\times A
under this isomorphism, therefore, the lines
l_{i}^{\mathbb{C}}
can be written as
l_{i}\times l_{i}
. Moreover, the
A\times A
-morphism
\varphi\colon l_{1}\times l_{1}\to(l_{2}\times l_{2})\otimes K_{X}
can be written as a pair of
A
-morphisms
\varphi_{1}\colon l_{1}\to l_{2}\otimes K_{X}
and
\varphi_{2}\colon l_{1}\to l_{2}\otimes K_{X}
. Analogously, the
A\times A
-morphism
\varphi^{\prime}\colon l_{1}\times l_{1}\to(l_{2}\times l_{2})\otimes K_{X}
can be written as a pair of
A
-morphisms
\varphi_{1}^{\prime}\colon l_{2}\to l_{1}\otimes K_{X}
and
\varphi_{2}^{\prime}\colon l_{2}\to l_{1}\otimes K_{X}
.

Proposition 11.15
.

The morphisms
\varphi_{1}
and
\varphi_{1}^{\prime}
determine the morphism
\varphi
uniquely.

Proof.

We fix a basis
(v_{1},v_{2})
of
A^{2}
such that
\omega(v_{1},v_{1})=-\omega(v_{2},v_{2})=1
and
l_{1}=v_{1}A
,
l_{2}=v_{2}A
, then

l_{1}^{\mathbb{C}}\cong l_{1}\times l_{1}=v_{1}(A\times A),\hskip 10.00002ptl_{2}^{\mathbb{C}}\cong l_{2}\times l_{2}=v_{2}(A\times A),

and

\varphi(v_{1},v_{1})=v_{2}(a_{1},a_{2})=(\varphi_{1}(v_{1}),\varphi_{2}(v_{1})),

for some
a_{1},a_{2}\in A
, and

\varphi^{\prime}(v_{2},v_{2})=v_{1}(a^{\prime}_{1},a^{\prime}_{2})=(\varphi^{\prime}_{1}(v_{2}),\varphi^{\prime}_{2}(v_{2})).

To prove the proposition, we need to show that
a_{2}
is uniquely determined by
a_{1}
and
a_{1}^{\prime}
. Further,

\displaystyle-(\bar{\sigma}(a_{2}),\bar{\sigma}(a_{1}))=\omega_{\mathbb{C}}(\varphi(v_{1}),v_{2})=\omega_{\mathbb{C}}(v_{1},\varphi^{\prime}(v_{2}))=(a^{\prime}_{1},a^{\prime}_{2}).

Therefore,
a_{2}=-\bar{\sigma}(a_{1}^{\prime})
.
∎

The proposition shows that we can use maps
\varphi_{1}
and
\varphi_{1}^{\prime}
instead of
\varphi
to define a Higgs field because they contain exactly the same information. Now we can give a definition of a
\mathrm{U}(n,n)
-Higgs bundle in terms of the projective space model.

Definition 11.16
.

A
\mathrm{U}(n,n)
-Higgs bundle
over
X
associated to a reductive representation
\rho\colon\pi_{1}(X)\to G
with a
\rho
-equivariant harmonic map
f\colon\tilde{X}\to\mathfrak{P}_{G}^{+}\cong G/K
is a holomorphic
\mathrm{GL}_{n}(\mathbb{C})\times\mathrm{GL}_{n}(\mathbb{C})
-principal bundle over
X
which can be seen as a pair
V_{1},V_{2}
of rank
n
holomorphic vector bundles over
X
. The Higgs field in terms of the projective space model is a holomorphic equivariant section of the pull-back bundle
f^{*}T^{\mathbb{C}}\mathfrak{P}_{G}^{+}\otimes K_{X}
which can be seen as a pair of holomorphic morphisms

\varphi\colon V_{1}\to V_{2}\otimes K_{X}
and
\varphi^{\prime}\colon V_{2}\to V_{1}\otimes K_{X}
.

Remark 11.17
.

Notice that this definition agrees with the definition of a
\mathrm{U}(n,n)
-Higgs bundle seen as an
\mathrm{Sp}_{2}(A,\sigma)
, for
(A,\sigma)=(\mathrm{Mat}_{n}(\mathbb{C}),\overline{\bullet}^{t})
(cf. Definition
10.11
).

11.3.4.
G=\mathrm{Sp}(n,n)

As seen in (
2.43
), the group
\mathrm{Sp}(n,n)
can be interpreted as the indefinite orthogonal group
\mathrm{O}_{(1,1)}(A:=\mathrm{Mat}_{n}(\mathbb{H}),\sigma_{1})
. From Section
3
, the complexified tangent space of the projective space model at a point
l\in\mathfrak{P}_{G}^{\pm}
is given by

\displaystyle T_{l}^{\mathbb{C}}\mathfrak{P}_{G}^{\pm}

\displaystyle=\{Q\colon l_{\mathbb{C}}\to l_{\mathbb{C}}^{\perp}\}.

The stabilizer of any
l\in\mathfrak{P}_{G}^{\pm}
is a maximal compact subgroup which is conjugated to
\mathrm{Sp}(n)\times\mathrm{Sp}(n)=\mathrm{O}(A,\sigma_{1})\times\mathrm{O}(A,\sigma_{1})
, where
\mathrm{O}(A,\sigma_{1})=\{a\in A^{\times}\mid\sigma_{1}(a)a=\mathrm{Id}\}
is the orthogonal group of
A
. Its complexification is
\mathrm{Sp}_{2n}(\mathbb{C})\times\mathrm{Sp}_{2n}(\mathbb{C})
. Using Definition
10.3
, we can directly give a definition of a
\mathrm{Sp}(n,n)
-Higgs bundle associated to a reductive representation
\rho\colon\pi_{1}(S)\to\mathrm{Sp}(n,n)
.

Definition 11.18
.

An
\mathrm{Sp}(n,n)
-Higgs bundle
over
X
is a triple
(V_{1},V_{2},\varphi)
, where
V_{i}
are two holomorphic vector bundles of rank
2n
equipped with symplectic forms
\omega_{i}
over
X
, and
\varphi\colon V_{1}\to V_{2}\otimes K_{X}
is a holomorphic morphism.

Remark 11.19
.

Since the forms
\omega_{1}
and
\omega_{2}
are non-degenerate, there is a unique morphism
\varphi^{\prime}\colon V_{2}\to V_{1}\otimes K_{X}
defined by

(11.20)

\omega_{1}(v_{1},\varphi^{\prime}(v_{2}))=\omega_{2}(\varphi(v_{1}),v_{2}),

for
v_{1}\in V_{1}
,
v_{2}\in V_{2}
.

11.3.5.
G=\mathrm{SO}^{*}(4n)

We realize the group
\mathrm{SO}^{*}(4n)
as an
\mathrm{Sp}_{2}(A,\sigma)
, for
A:=\mathrm{Mat}_{n}(\mathbb{H})
with the Hermitian anti-involution
\sigma:=\sigma_{1}
. The complexified algebra
A_{\mathbb{C}}
is isomorphic to
\mathrm{Mat}_{2n}(\mathbb{C})
, the complex linear extension
\sigma_{\mathbb{C}}
of
\sigma
corresponds to the following map:

m\mapsto-\begin{pmatrix}0&\mathrm{Id}\\
-\mathrm{Id}&0\end{pmatrix}m^{t}\begin{pmatrix}0&\mathrm{Id}\\
-\mathrm{Id}&0\end{pmatrix}\ \ (\text{cf.~\cite[cite]{[\@@bibref{}{ABRRW}{}{}, Appendix B.1.2]}}).

In particular,

A_{\mathbb{C}}^{\sigma_{\mathbb{C}}}=\left\{\begin{pmatrix}a&b\\
c&a^{t}\end{pmatrix}\,\middle|\,a\in\mathrm{Mat}_{n}(\mathbb{C}),\;b,c\in\mathrm{Skew}_{n}(\mathbb{C})\right\}.

By (
6.18
), the complexified tangent space of the projective space model at a point
l\in\mathfrak{P}_{G}^{\pm}
is given by

\displaystyle T_{l}^{\mathbb{C}}\mathfrak{P}_{G}^{\pm}

\displaystyle=\left\{(Q,Q^{\prime})\in\mathrm{Hom}(l,\bar{l})\times\mathrm{Hom}(\bar{l},l)\,\middle|\,\begin{aligned} &\ \omega_{\mathbb{C}}(Q(x),x)\in A^{\sigma_{\mathbb{C}}}_{\mathbb{C}},\\
&\ \omega_{\mathbb{C}}(Q^{\prime}(x),x)\in A^{\sigma_{\mathbb{C}}}_{\mathbb{C}}\end{aligned}\right\}

\displaystyle=T_{l}\mathfrak{P}_{G}^{\pm}\oplus T_{\bar{l}}\mathfrak{P}_{G}^{\mp}.

Notice that the condition
\omega_{\mathbb{C}}(x,y)=\sigma_{\mathbb{C}}(x)^{t}\begin{pmatrix}0&\mathrm{Id}\\
-\mathrm{Id}&0\end{pmatrix}y\in A^{\sigma_{\mathbb{C}}}_{\mathbb{C}}
, where
x,y\in A_{\mathbb{C}}^{2}
, is equivalent to

\omega^{\prime}(x,y)=x^{t}\begin{pmatrix}0&0&0&\mathrm{Id}\\
0&0&-\mathrm{Id}&0\\
0&-\mathrm{Id}&0&0\\
\mathrm{Id}&0&0&0\\
\end{pmatrix}y\in\mathrm{Skew}_{2n}(\mathbb{C}).

The stabilizer of any
l\in\mathfrak{P}_{G}^{\pm}
is a maximal compact subgroup which is conjugated to
\mathrm{U}(2n)
. Its complexification is
\mathrm{GL}_{2n}(\mathbb{C})
. Using Definition
10.11
, we can directly give a definition of a
\mathrm{SO}^{*}(4n)
-Higgs bundle associated to a reductive representation
\rho\colon\pi_{1}(S)\to\mathrm{SO}^{*}(4n)
.

Definition 11.21
.

An
\mathrm{SO}^{*}(4n)
-Higgs bundle
over
X
is a tuple
(V_{1},V_{2},\omega,\varphi_{1},\varphi_{2})
, where
V_{1}
,
V_{2}
are holomorphic vector bundles of rank
2n
over
X
,
\omega
is a non-degenerate pairing between
V_{1}
and
V_{2}
, i.e.
\omega\colon V_{1}\otimes V_{2}\to\mathbb{C}
, and
\varphi_{1}\colon V_{1}\to V_{2}\otimes K_{X}
and
