\varphi_{2}\colon V_{2}\to V_{1}\otimes K_{X}
are two holomorphic
\omega
-skew-symmetric morphisms, i.e. forms
\omega(\cdot,\varphi_{2}(\cdot))
and
\omega(\varphi_{1}(\cdot),\cdot)
are skew-symmetric on
V_{1}
resp.
V_{2}
.

Remark 11.22
.

Since
\omega
provides the bundle isomorphism
V_{2}\cong V_{1}^{*}
, the above Definition
11.21
is equivalent to the classical one given in
[
6
, Section 4.2]
, that is, an
\mathrm{SO}^{*}(4n)
-Higgs bundle over
X
is a triple
(V,\varphi_{1},\varphi_{2})
with
V
a holomorphic bundle of rank
2n
, and
\varphi_{1}:V\to V^{*}\otimes K_{X}
and
\varphi_{2}:V^{*}\to V\otimes K_{X}
are two skew-symmetric morphisms.

11.3.6.
G=\mathrm{SO}_{0}(2,n)
and
G=E_{7{(-25)}}

The groups
G=\mathrm{SO}_{0}(2,n)
and
G=E_{7(-25)}
are also Hermitian of tube type, and classical Higgs bundle definitions for these have been given in
[
6
]
and
[
5
]
, respectively. Note, however, that these groups cannot be described as groups of the form
\mathrm{Sp}_{2}(A,\sigma)
for a Hermitian algebra
(A,\sigma)
. The group
\mathrm{Spin}_{0}(2,n)
, which is a double cover of
\mathrm{SO}_{0}(2,n)
, does admit a realization as a symplectic group
\mathrm{Sp}_{2}
over a certain Hermitian submonoid of the Clifford algebra of signature
(1,n-1)
. Models of the symmetric space for
\mathrm{Spin}_{0}(2,n)
still exist and have been described in
[
16
, Section 2.10]
. However, the description of the complexified tangent bundle of these symmetric spaces lies beyond the scope of this article. As for the group
E_{7(-25)}
, no realization in terms of
\mathrm{Sp}_{2}
, nor models of its symmetric space within this framework, have been provided in the literature to date.

12.
Hitchin morphism

In this section, we consider the Hitchin morphism for moduli spaces of polystable
G
-Higgs bundles for the definitions obtained in the previous sections. Using the various models of the symmetric space associated to the groups
G
, we give alternative, more geometric interpretations of this morphism and its image.

12.1.
The case
G=\mathrm{SL}_{2}(\mathbb{C})

The complex Lie group
G=\mathrm{SL}_{2}(\mathbb{C})
can be viewed as a symplectic group
\mathrm{Sp}_{2}(A_{\mathbb{C}},\sigma_{\mathbb{C}})
for
A=\mathbb{R}
and
\sigma=\mathrm{Id}
. Then,
K=\mathrm{Sp}(1)\cong\mathrm{SU}(2)
and
\mathfrak{m}=\mathbb{H}^{\sigma_{0}}
with complexification
\mathfrak{m}^{\mathbb{C}}=\mathbb{H}^{\sigma_{0}}\otimes\mathbb{C}
. There is a map

(12.1)

\displaystyle\mathbb{H}^{\sigma_{0}}

\displaystyle\to\mathbb{R}^{+}

\displaystyle q

\displaystyle\mapsto|q|^{2}=q\bar{q}

defined as the square of the norm of a quaternionic element. This map is preserved by the action of
\mathrm{SU}(2)
on
\mathbb{H}^{\sigma_{0}}
. Therefore, the map above is invariant for this action. Now take the complex extension of the map (
12.1
) given by

\mathbb{H}^{\sigma_{0}}\otimes_{\mathbb{R}}\mathbb{C}\to\mathbb{C}

and this is invariant under the action of
K^{\mathbb{C}}
, the complexification of
\mathrm{SU}(2)
.

Quaternionic structures model.
In this model, an
\mathrm{SL}_{2}(\mathbb{C})
-Higgs field is given by an element
L\in T_{J}^{\mathbb{C}}\mathfrak{C}_{G}
with

JL+LJ=0.

Since
L\in\mathrm{Mat}_{2}(\mathbb{C})
, the coefficient of its invariant polynomial is given by the trace
\mathrm{Tr}(L^{2})
. Therefore, in this model, the Hitchin base is no different than the classical description.

Projective space model.
Now we consider
\mathrm{SL}_{2}(\mathbb{C})
-Higgs bundles in the projective space model. For
l\in\mathfrak{P}_{G}
and
Q\in T_{l}\mathfrak{P}_{G}
, it is
Q\colon l\to\theta_{j}(l)
. The map

Q^{\prime}:=\theta_{j}\circ Q\circ\theta_{j}\colon\theta_{j}(l)\mapsto l

is
\mathbb{H}
-linear. The pair of lines
(l,\theta_{j}(l))
give rise to a symplectic basis
(v,v^{\prime}:=\theta_{j}(v)j)
of
\mathbb{H}^{2}
such that
v
generates
l
and
v^{\prime}
generates
\theta_{j}(l)
. Then
Q(v)=v^{\prime}a
and
Q^{\prime}(v^{\prime})=v\theta_{i}(\theta_{j}(a))=v\bar{a}
, for
a\in\mathbb{H}^{\sigma_{0}}
, where
\bar{a}
is a quaternionic conjugate of
a
. Consider the operator

Q^{\prime}\circ Q\colon l\mapsto l.

Its operator norm

\|Q^{\prime}\circ Q\|:=\sup_{x\in l,\,\|x\|=1}\|(Q^{\prime}\circ Q)(x)\|=\bar{a}a\in\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}

does not depend on the choice of the symplectic basis
(v,v^{\prime})
, in other words, this operator norm is invariant under the action of
\mathrm{SL}_{2}(\mathbb{C})
on the tangent bundle. In particular, for every
l\in\mathfrak{P}_{G}
, this is an invariant of the action of
\mathrm{Stab}_{\mathrm{SL}_{2}(\mathbb{C})}(l)\cong\mathrm{SU}(2)
.

Now for
Q\in T_{l}^{\mathbb{C}}\mathfrak{P}_{G}
, it is
Q\colon l\otimes\mathbb{C}\{I\}\mapsto\theta_{j}(l)\otimes\mathbb{C}\{I\}
and
Q^{\prime}:=\theta_{j}\circ Q\circ\theta_{j}
. In a symplectic basis
(v,v^{\prime}:=\theta_{j}(v)j)
of
\mathbb{H}^{2}
such that
v
generates
l
and
v^{\prime}
generates
\theta_{j}(l)
,
Q(v)=v^{\prime}a
and
Q^{\prime}(v^{\prime})=v\theta_{i}(\theta_{j}(a))
, for
a\in\mathbb{H}^{\sigma_{0}}\otimes\mathbb{C}\{I\}
. The assignment

\begin{array}[]{rccl}\mathcal{N}_{\mathbb{C}}\colon&T^{\mathbb{C}}\mathfrak{P}_{G}^{+}&\to&\mathbb{C}\{I\}\\
&(l,Q)&\mapsto&\theta_{i}(\theta_{j}(a))a\end{array}

gives an invariant under the action of
\mathrm{SL}_{2}(\mathbb{C})
seen as the complexification of
\mathrm{Stab}_{\mathrm{SL}_{2}(\mathbb{C})}(l)
.

Then this complexified norm map is invariant under the action of
K^{\mathbb{C}}
by symplectic transformations. Therefore, we get a map

f^{*}_{\mathfrak{P}_{G}^{+}}T^{\mathbb{C}}\mathfrak{P}_{G}^{+}\otimes K_{X}\to H^{0}(X,K^{2}_{X})

which maps a point
(E_{K^{\mathbb{C}}},\Phi=(l,Q)dz)
in
\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{Higgs}}(X,\mathrm{SL}_{2}(\mathbb{C}))
to
\mathcal{N}_{\mathbb{C}}(l,Q)dz^{2}
.

Upper half-space and pre-compact model.
We consider an
\mathrm{SL}_{2}(\mathbb{C})
-Higgs bundle in the upper half-space model or in the pre-compact model:

\mathfrak{U}_{G}\cong\mathfrak{B}_{G}.

In these two cases, we have

T^{\mathbb{C}}\mathfrak{U}\cong T^{\mathbb{C}}\mathfrak{B}_{G}\cong A_{\mathbb{H}}^{\sigma_{0}}\otimes_{\mathbb{R}}\mathbb{C}\cong\mathbb{H}^{\sigma_{0}}\otimes_{\mathbb{R}}\mathbb{C}.

Moreover, the Higgs field
\Phi
is a
(1,0)
-form with values in

f^{*}_{\mathfrak{U}}T^{\mathbb{C}}\mathfrak{U}\cong f^{*}_{\mathfrak{B}_{G}}T^{\mathbb{C}}\mathfrak{B}_{G},

which is an adjoint
\mathrm{SU}(2)
-bundle.

We next define a morphism
\mathcal{N}_{\mathbb{C}}\colon T^{\mathbb{C}}\mathfrak{U}\to\mathbb{C}
. For
(z,v)\in T^{\mathbb{C}}\mathfrak{U}
, there exists
g\in\mathrm{SL}_{2}(\mathbb{C})
such that
g.(z,v)=(i,v_{0})
, for
v_{0}\in\mathbb{H}^{\sigma_{0}}\otimes_{\mathbb{R}}\mathbb{C}\{I\}
. Let
\mathcal{N}_{\mathbb{C}}(z,v):=\theta_{i}(\theta_{j}(v_{0}))v_{0}\in\mathbb{C}
. This is invariant under the action of the complexified

\mathrm{Stab}_{\mathrm{SL}_{2}(\mathbb{C})}(z)
on
T_{z}^{\mathbb{C}}\mathfrak{U}
which is isomorphic to
\mathrm{SL}_{2}(\mathbb{C})
which is the complexified stabilizer of the point
i
.

The construction for
\mathfrak{B}_{G}
is similar. The only difference is that we use the point
0\in\mathfrak{B}_{G}
instead of
i\in\mathfrak{U}_{G}
.

The morphism
\mathcal{N}_{\mathbb{C}}
introduced above extends to the map

f^{*}_{\mathfrak{U}}T^{\mathbb{C}}\mathfrak{U}\otimes K_{X}\to H^{0}(X,K^{2}_{X}),

which maps a point
(E_{K^{\mathbb{C}}},\Phi=(z,v)dz)
in
\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{Higgs}}(X,\mathrm{SL}_{2}(\mathbb{C}))
to
\mathcal{N}_{\mathbb{C}}(z,v)dz^{2}
. This defines the Hitchin morphism for these two models of the symmetric space.

12.2.
The general case
G=\mathrm{Sp}_{2}(A_{\mathbb{C}},\sigma_{\mathbb{C}})

We now give the description of the Hitchin base for general complex symplectic groups, motivated by the descriptions in the case of
\mathrm{SL}_{2}(\mathbb{C})
above.

Before we start discussing the Hitchin morphism in different models, we introduce and discuss some properties of the following
\mathrm{KSp}^{c}_{2}(A_{\mathbb{C}},\sigma_{\mathbb{C}})
-equivariant map:

\displaystyle\tilde{\mathcal{N}}_{\mathbb{R}}\colon A_{\mathbb{H}}^{\sigma_{0}}

\displaystyle\to(A_{\mathbb{H}}^{\sigma_{1}})_{\geq 0}

\displaystyle q

\displaystyle\mapsto q\sigma_{1}(q),

where
\mathrm{KSp}^{c}_{2}(A_{\mathbb{C}},\sigma_{\mathbb{C}})
acts on
A^{\sigma_{1}}_{\mathbb{H}}
by conjugation preserving
(A_{\mathbb{H}}^{\sigma_{1}})_{\geq 0}
, and on
A^{\sigma_{0}}_{\mathbb{H}}
by
\sigma_{0}
-congruence. More precisely, for
k=\begin{pmatrix}a&b\\
-\bar{b}&\bar{a}\end{pmatrix}\in\mathrm{KSp}^{c}_{2}(A_{\mathbb{C}},\sigma_{\mathbb{C}})
which we also identify with the
a+bj\in A_{\mathbb{H}}
such that
\bar{\sigma}_{\mathbb{C}}(a)a+\sigma_{\mathbb{C}}(b)\bar{b}=1\text{ and }\bar{\sigma}_{\mathbb{C}}(a)b-\sigma_{\mathbb{C}}(b)\bar{a}=0,\text{ for }a,b\in A_{\mathbb{C}}
, the action on
A_{\mathbb{H}}^{\sigma_{0}}
is given by

k.q=kq\sigma_{0}(k)=(a+bj)q(-\bar{b}j+\bar{a})^{-1},

for
q\in A_{\mathbb{H}}^{\sigma_{0}}
.
Moreover, it is

\displaystyle k.q\,\sigma_{1}(k.q)

\displaystyle=(a+bj)q\left(\sigma_{\mathbb{C}}(a)+\bar{\sigma}_{\mathbb{C}}(b)j\right)(\bar{a}-\bar{b}j)\sigma_{1}(q)\left(\bar{\sigma}_{\mathbb{C}}(a)-\sigma_{\mathbb{C}}(b)j\right)

\displaystyle=(a+bj)q\sigma_{1}(q)\left(\bar{\sigma}_{\mathbb{C}}(a)-\sigma_{\mathbb{C}}(b)j\right)

\displaystyle=(a+bj)q\sigma_{1}(q)(a+bj)^{-1}.

This implies that the map is indeed equivariant.
We introduce the quotient map:

(12.2)

\begin{array}[]{rccl}\mathcal{N}_{\mathbb{R}}\colon&A^{\sigma_{0}}_{\mathbb{H}}&\to&A^{\sigma_{1}}_{\mathbb{H}}/\mathrm{KSp}^{c}_{2}(A_{\mathbb{C}},\sigma_{\mathbb{C}})\\
&q&\mapsto&[q\sigma_{1}(q)].\end{array}

After the complexification of
A_{\mathbb{H}}
, we can define the corresponding complexifying map:

(12.3)

\begin{array}[]{rrcl}\mathcal{N}_{\mathbb{C}}\colon&A^{\sigma_{0}}_{\mathbb{H}}\otimes_{\mathbb{R}}\mathbb{C}\{I\}&\to&A^{\sigma_{1}}_{\mathbb{H}}\otimes_{\mathbb{R}}\mathbb{C}\{I\}/\!\!/\mathrm{Sp}_{2}(A_{\mathbb{C}},\sigma_{\mathbb{C}})\\
&q&\mapsto&[q\sigma_{1}(q)],\end{array}

where we, slightly abusing the notation, denote the
\mathbb{C}\{I\}
-linear extension of
\sigma_{1}
again by
\sigma_{1}
and
\mathrm{Sp}_{2}(A_{\mathbb{C}},\sigma_{\mathbb{C}})
is seen as the complexification of
\mathrm{KSp}^{c}_{2}(A_{\mathbb{C}},\sigma_{\mathbb{C}})
. This action is, as before, invariant under
\sigma_{0}
-congruence where we, also slightly abusing the notation, denote the
\mathbb{C}\{I\}
-linear extension of
\sigma_{0}
again by
\sigma_{0}
. Notice that because the group is not compact, the affine GIT-quotient is needed to provide a quotient which is Hausdorff and, more generally, an algebraic variety. The map
\mathcal{N}_{\mathbb{C}}
is known as the affine GIT morphism and, furthermore, by the Chevalley restriction theorem, this GIT-quotient can be identified with
\mathfrak{a}^{\mathbb{C}}/W(\mathfrak{a}^{\mathbb{C}})
where
\mathfrak{a}^{\mathbb{C}}
is a maximal (abelian) subalgebra in
\mathfrak{sp}_{2}(A_{\mathbb{C}},\sigma_{\mathbb{C}})
, and
W(\mathfrak{a}^{\mathbb{C}})
is the Weyl group associated to
\mathfrak{a}^{\mathbb{C}}
(cf.
[
12
, Section 4]
).

The invariants of elements in
A^{\sigma_{1}}_{\mathbb{H}}\otimes_{\mathbb{R}}\mathbb{C}\{I\}
under the action of
\mathrm{Sp}_{2}(A_{\mathbb{C}},\sigma_{\mathbb{C}})
are given by coefficients of the characteristic polynomials which are homogeneous polynomials of degrees
d\in\{d_{1},...,d_{n}\}
of the complexification of the formally real Jordan algebra
A_{\mathbb{H}}^{\sigma_{1}}
. Thus, for each
d
there is a map defined by the polynomial of degree
d

\mathcal{N}_{\mathbb{C}}^{d}\colon A_{\mathbb{H}}^{\sigma_{0}}\otimes_{\mathbb{R}}\mathbb{C}\{I\}\to\mathbb{C}\{I\}.

Quaternionic structures model.
As seen above in the special case of
\mathrm{SL}_{2}(\mathbb{C})
in this model, the coefficient of the invariant polynomial of an element
L
in the complexified tangent
T^{\mathbb{C}}_{J}\mathfrak{C}_{G}
is just given by the trace
\mathrm{Tr}(L^{2})
. For the case when the algebra
A
is a matrix algebra, we can define analogously a map from general
\mathrm{Sp}_{2}(A_{\mathbb{C}},\sigma_{\mathbb{C}})
-Higgs bundles to the invariant polynomials of their Higgs field viewed as an element in
T^{\mathbb{C}}_{J}\mathfrak{C}_{G}
given by the traces of all powers of a point
L
viewed as a
(2\times 2)
-matrix over
A_{\mathbb{C}}
. Note that the requirement that
A
is a matrix algebra is not necessary for the realization of the Hitchin
\mathrm{Sp}_{2}(A_{\mathbb{C}},\sigma_{\mathbb{C}})
-morphism in the other models.

Projective space model.

Now we consider the general case of
\mathrm{Sp}_{2}(A_{\mathbb{C}},\sigma_{\mathbb{C}})
-Higgs bundles in the projective space model. An element
Q\in T_{l}\mathfrak{P}_{G}
of the tangent space at
l\in\mathfrak{P}_{G}
can be seen as an
A_{\mathbb{H}}
-linear map
Q\colon l\mapsto\theta_{j}(l)
. The map

Q^{\prime}:=\theta_{j}\circ Q\circ\theta_{j}\colon\theta_{j}(l)\mapsto l

is
A_{\mathbb{H}}
-linear. The pair of lines
(l,\theta_{j}(l))
give rise to a symplectic basis
(v,v^{\prime}:=\theta_{j}(v)j)
of
A_{\mathbb{H}}^{2}
such that
v
generates
l
and
v^{\prime}
generates
\theta_{j}(l)
. Then
Q(v)=v^{\prime}a
and
Q^{\prime}(v^{\prime})=v\theta_{i}(\theta_{j}(a))=v\sigma_{1}(a)
, for
a\in A_{\mathbb{H}}^{\sigma_{0}}
.
We define an
A_{\mathbb{H}}^{\sigma_{1}}
-valued map which is an
A_{\mathbb{H}}^{\sigma_{1}}
-valued norm of
Q^{\prime}\circ Q
:

\tilde{\mathcal{N}}_{\mathbb{R}}^{\mathfrak{P}}(Q):=a\sigma_{1}(a)\in(A_{\mathbb{H}}^{\sigma_{1}})_{\geq 0}.

This is equivariant under the actions of
\mathrm{KSp}^{c}_{2}(A_{\mathbb{C}},\sigma_{\mathbb{C}})
on
A_{\mathbb{H}}^{\sigma_{1}}
by conjugation and
as

\mathrm{Stab}_{\mathrm{Sp}_{2}(A_{\mathbb{C}},\sigma_{\mathbb{C}})}(l)=g\mathrm{KSp}^{c}_{2}(A_{\mathbb{C}},\sigma_{\mathbb{C}})g^{-1}

on
T_{l}\mathfrak{P}_{G}
, for some fixed
g\in\mathrm{Sp}_{2}(A_{\mathbb{C}},\sigma_{\mathbb{C}})
such that
g((j,1)^{t}A_{\mathbb{H}})=l
. Thus, it descends (cf. (
12.2
)) to the map

{\mathcal{N}}_{\mathbb{R}}^{\mathfrak{P}}\colon T_{l}\mathfrak{P}_{G}\to A^{\sigma_{1}}_{\mathbb{H}}/\mathrm{KSp}^{c}_{2}(A_{\mathbb{C}},\sigma_{0}).

Now, for
Q\in T_{l}^{\mathbb{C}}\mathfrak{P}_{G}
, it is
Q\colon l\otimes\mathbb{C}\{I\}\mapsto\theta_{j}(l)\otimes\mathbb{C}\{I\}
and
Q^{\prime}:=\theta_{j}\circ Q\circ\theta_{j}
. In a symplectic basis
(v,v^{\prime}:=\theta_{j}(v)j)
of
A_{\mathbb{H}}^{2}
such that
v
generates
l
and
v^{\prime}
generates
\theta_{j}(l)
,
Q(v)=v^{\prime}a
and
Q^{\prime}(v^{\prime})=v\theta_{i}(\theta_{j}(a))=v\sigma_{1}(a)
, for
a\in A_{\mathbb{H}}^{\sigma_{0}}\otimes\mathbb{C}\{I\}
. The assignment

\begin{array}[]{rccl}\mathcal{N}_{\mathbb{C}}^{\mathfrak{P}}\colon&T^{\mathbb{C}}\mathfrak{P}_{G}&\to&(A_{\mathbb{H}}^{\sigma_{1}})\otimes_{\mathbb{R}}\mathbb{C}\{I\}/\!\!/\mathrm{Sp}_{2}(A_{\mathbb{C}},\sigma_{\mathbb{C}})\\
&(l,Q)&\mapsto&[a\sigma_{1}(a)]\end{array}

gives an invariant under the action of
\mathrm{Sp}_{2}(A_{\mathbb{C}},\sigma_{\mathbb{C}})
.

Therefore, for all
d\in\{d_{1},\dots,d_{n}\}
as above, we get a map

\hat{\mathcal{N}}^{d}_{\mathbb{C}}\colon\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{Higgs}}(X,\mathrm{Sp}_{2}(A_{\mathbb{C}},\sigma_{\mathbb{C}}))\to H^{0}(X,K^{2d}_{X}),

which maps a point
(E_{K^{\mathbb{C}}},\Phi=(l,Q)dz)
in
\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{Higgs}}(X,\mathrm{Sp}_{2}(A_{\mathbb{C}},\sigma_{\mathbb{C}}))
to
\hat{\mathcal{N}}^{d}_{\mathbb{C}}(l,Q)dz^{2d}
.
This then finally induces a map

\displaystyle\hat{\mathcal{N}}_{\mathbb{C}}\colon\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{Higgs}}(X,\mathrm{Sp}_{2}(A_{\mathbb{C}},\sigma_{\mathbb{C}}))

\displaystyle\to\bigoplus_{i=1}^{n}H^{0}(X,K_{X}^{2d_{i}}).

Upper half-space and pre-compact model.
In this case, it is

T^{\mathbb{C}}_{\bullet}\mathfrak{U}_{G}\cong T^{\mathbb{C}}_{\bullet}\mathfrak{B}_{G}\cong A_{\mathbb{H}}^{\sigma_{0}}\otimes_{\mathbb{R}}\mathbb{C}.
We define the morphism

\mathcal{N}_{\mathbb{C}}^{\mathfrak{U}}\colon T^{\mathbb{C}}\mathfrak{U}\to(A_{\mathbb{H}}^{\sigma_{1}})\otimes_{\mathbb{R}}\mathbb{C}\{I\}/\!\!/\mathrm{Sp}_{2}(A_{\mathbb{C}},\sigma_{\mathbb{C}})

as follows. For
(z,v)\in T^{\mathbb{C}}\mathfrak{U}
, there exists
g\in\mathrm{Sp}_{2}(A_{\mathbb{C}},\sigma_{\mathbb{C}})
such that
g.(z,v)=(i,v_{0})
, for
v_{0}\in A_{\mathbb{H}}^{\sigma_{0}}\otimes_{\mathbb{R}}\mathbb{C}\{I\}
. Let

\mathcal{N}_{\mathbb{C}}(z,v):=[v_{0}\sigma_{1}(v_{0})]\in A_{\mathbb{H}}^{\sigma_{0}}\otimes\mathbb{C}\{I\}/\mathrm{Sp}_{2}(A_{\mathbb{C}},\sigma_{\mathbb{C}}).

This is an invariant under the action of the complexified

\mathrm{Stab}_{\mathrm{Sp}_{2}(A_{\mathbb{C}},\sigma_{\mathbb{C}})}(z)
on
T_{z}^{\mathbb{C}}\mathfrak{U}
which is isomorphic to
\mathrm{Sp}_{2}(A_{\mathbb{C}},\sigma_{\mathbb{C}})
which is the complexified stabilizer of the point
i
.

The construction for
\mathfrak{B}_{G}
is similar. The only difference is that we use the point
0\in\mathfrak{B}_{G}
instead of
i\in\mathfrak{U}_{G}
.

As above, the invariants of elements in
A^{\sigma_{1}}_{\mathbb{H}}\otimes_{\mathbb{R}}\mathbb{C}\{I\}
under the action of
\mathrm{Sp}_{2}(A_{\mathbb{C}},\sigma_{\mathbb{C}})
are given by coefficients of the characteristic polynomials which are homogeneous polynomials of degrees
d\in\{d_{1},...,d_{n}\}
of the complexification of the formally real Jordan algebra
A_{\mathbb{H}}^{\sigma_{1}}
. Thus, for each
d
there is a map defined by the polynomial of degree
d
:

\mathcal{N}_{\mathbb{C}}^{d}\colon A_{\mathbb{H}}^{\sigma_{0}}\otimes_{\mathbb{R}}\mathbb{C}\to\mathbb{C}

and now this map for each
d
induces the map

\displaystyle\hat{\mathcal{N}}_{\mathbb{C}}^{d}\colon\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{Higgs}}(X,\mathrm{Sp}_{2}(A_{\mathbb{C}},\sigma_{\mathbb{C}}))

\displaystyle\to H^{0}(X,K_{X}^{2d}).

This then finally induces a Hitchin morphism

\displaystyle\hat{\mathcal{N}}_{\mathbb{C}}\colon\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{Higgs}}(X,\mathrm{Sp}_{2}(A_{\mathbb{C}},\sigma_{\mathbb{C}}))

\displaystyle\to\bigoplus_{i=1}^{n}H^{0}(X,K_{X}^{2d_{i}}).

12.3.
The case
G=\mathrm{O}(A_{\mathbb{C}},\sigma_{\mathbb{C}})

Similarly to the complex group
\mathrm{Sp}_{2}(A_{\mathbb{C}},\sigma_{\mathbb{C}})
, we now give the description of the Hitchin base for the complex groups
\mathrm{O}(A_{\mathbb{C}},\sigma_{\mathbb{C}})
realized as a subgroup
\hat{G}
of
\mathrm{O}_{(1,1)}(A,\sigma)
(cf. Section
5
).

Before we start discussing the Hitchin morphism in different models, we introduce and discuss some properties of the following
\mathrm{O}(A,\sigma)
-equivariant map:

\displaystyle\tilde{\mathcal{N}}_{\mathbb{R}}\colon A^{-\sigma}

\displaystyle\to(A^{\sigma})_{\geq 0}

\displaystyle q

\displaystyle\mapsto q\sigma(q)=-q^{2},

where
\mathrm{O}(A,\sigma)
acts by conjugation on
A^{\sigma}
and on
A^{-\sigma}
; note that the conjugation preserves
(A^{\sigma})_{\geq 0}
inside
A^{\sigma}
. We introduce the quotient map:

(12.4)

\begin{array}[]{rccl}\mathcal{N}_{\mathbb{R}}\colon&A^{-\sigma}&\to&A^{\sigma}/\mathrm{O}(A,\sigma)\\
&q&\mapsto&[-q^{2}].\end{array}

After the complexification of
A
, we can define the corresponding complexifying map:

(12.5)

\begin{array}[]{rrcl}\mathcal{N}_{\mathbb{C}}\colon&A^{-\sigma_{\mathbb{C}}}_{\mathbb{C}}&\to&A^{\sigma_{\mathbb{C}}}_{\mathbb{C}}/\!\!/\mathrm{O}(A_{\mathbb{C}},\sigma_{\mathbb{C}})\\
&q&\mapsto&[-q^{2}],\end{array}

where
\mathrm{O}(A_{\mathbb{C}},\sigma_{\mathbb{C}})
is seen as the complexification of
\mathrm{O}(A,\sigma)
. This action is, as before, invariant under conjugation. Notice that because the group is not compact, the affine GIT-quotient is needed in order to provide a quotient which is Hausdorff and, more generally, an algebraic variety. The map
\mathcal{N}_{\mathbb{C}}
is known as the affine GIT morphism and, furthermore, by the Chevalley restriction theorem, this GIT-quotient can be identified with
\mathfrak{a}^{\mathbb{C}}/W(\mathfrak{a}^{\mathbb{C}})
where
\mathfrak{a}^{\mathbb{C}}
is a maximal (abelian) subalgebra in
\mathfrak{o}(A_{\mathbb{C}},\sigma_{\mathbb{C}})
and
W(\mathfrak{a}^{\mathbb{C}})
is the Weyl group associated to
\mathfrak{a}^{\mathbb{C}}
(cf.
[
12
, Section 4]
).

The invariants of elements in
A^{\sigma_{\mathbb{C}}}_{\mathbb{C}}
under the action of
\mathrm{O}(A_{\mathbb{C}},\sigma_{\mathbb{C}})
are given by coefficients of the characteristic polynomials which are homogeneous polynomials of degree
d\in\{d_{1},...,d_{n}\}
of the complexification of the formally real Jordan algebra
A^{\sigma}
. Thus, for each
d
there is a map defined by the polynomial of degree
d

\mathcal{N}_{\mathbb{C}}^{d}\colon A_{\mathbb{C}}^{-\sigma_{\mathbb{C}}}\to\mathbb{C}.

Indefinite involutive operators model.

For the special case when
A
is a matrix algebra, we can define analogously a map from general
\mathrm{O}(A_{\mathbb{C}},\sigma_{\mathbb{C}})
-Higgs bundles as in Definition
10.7
to the coefficients of the characteristic polynomials of their Higgs field viewed as an element in
T^{\mathbb{C}}_{J}\mathfrak{C}_{G}
given by the traces of all powers of the operator
L
viewed as a
(2\times 2)
-matrix over
A_{\mathbb{C}}
. Note that the requirement that
A
is a matrix algebra is not necessary for the realization of the Hitchin
\mathrm{O}(A_{\mathbb{C}},\sigma_{\mathbb{C}})
-morphism in the other models.

Projective space model.

Now we consider the case of
\mathrm{O}(A_{\mathbb{C}},\sigma_{\mathbb{C}})
-Higgs bundles in the projective space model. An element
Q\in T_{l}^{\mathbb{C}}\mathfrak{P}_{G}
of the complexified tangent space at
l\in\mathfrak{P}_{G}
can be seen as a
A_{\mathbb{C}}
-linear map
Q\colon l\mapsto l
, such that
h_{\mathbb{C}}(Q(x),x)\in A_{\mathbb{C}}^{-\sigma_{\mathbb{C}}}
(cf. (
5.4
)). We can choose
x\in l
so that
h_{\mathbb{C}}(x,x)=1
. Then,
Q(x)=xa
, for
a\in A_{\mathbb{C}}^{-\sigma_{\mathbb{C}}}
.

We define an
A_{\mathbb{C}}^{\sigma_{\mathbb{C}}}
-valued map

\tilde{\mathcal{N}}_{\mathbb{C}}^{\mathfrak{P}}(Q):=a\sigma_{\mathbb{C}}(a)\in(A_{\mathbb{C}}^{\sigma_{\mathbb{C}}})_{\geq 0},

which is equivariant under the actions of
\mathrm{O}(A_{\mathbb{C}},\sigma_{\mathbb{C}})
on
A_{\mathbb{C}}^{\sigma_{\mathbb{C}}}
(seen as the complexification of
\mathrm{O}(A,\sigma)
, which is a maximal compact subgroup of
\mathrm{O}(A_{\mathbb{C}},\sigma_{\mathbb{C}})
) by conjugation and
as the complexification of
\mathrm{Stab}_{\mathrm{O}(A_{\mathbb{C}},\sigma_{\mathbb{C}})}(l)=g\mathrm{O}(A,\sigma)g^{-1}
on
T_{l}\mathfrak{P}_{G}
, for some fixed
g\in\mathrm{O}(A_{\mathbb{C}},\sigma_{\mathbb{C}})
such that
g((1,1)^{t}A)=l
. Thus, it descends to the map
{\mathcal{N}}_{\mathbb{C}}^{\mathfrak{P}}\colon T_{l}\mathfrak{P}_{G}\to A^{\sigma_{\mathbb{C}}}_{\mathbb{C}}/\!\!/\mathrm{O}(A_{\mathbb{C}},\sigma_{0})
(cf. (
12.5
)). The assignment

\begin{array}[]{rccl}\mathcal{N}_{\mathbb{C}}^{\mathfrak{P}}\colon&T^{\mathbb{C}}\mathfrak{P}_{G}&\to&(A_{\mathbb{C}}^{\sigma_{\mathbb{C}}})/\!\!/\mathrm{O}(A_{\mathbb{C}},\sigma_{\mathbb{C}})\\
&(l,Q)&\mapsto&[a\sigma_{\mathbb{C}}(a)]\end{array}

gives an invariant under the action of
\mathrm{Sp}_{2}(A_{\mathbb{C}},\sigma_{\mathbb{C}})
.

Therefore, for all
d\in\{d_{1},\dots,d_{n}\}
as above, we get a map

\hat{\mathcal{N}}^{d}_{\mathbb{C}}\colon\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{Higgs}}(X,\mathrm{O}(A_{\mathbb{C}},\sigma_{\mathbb{C}}))\to H^{0}(X,K^{2d}_{X})

which maps a point
(E_{K^{\mathbb{C}}},\Phi=(l,Q)dz)
in
\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{Higgs}}(X,\mathrm{Sp}_{2}(A_{\mathbb{C}},\sigma_{\mathbb{C}}))
to
\hat{\mathcal{N}}^{d}_{\mathbb{C}}(l,Q)dz^{2d}
.
This then finally induces a map

\displaystyle\hat{\mathcal{N}}_{\mathbb{C}}\colon\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{Higgs}}(X,\mathrm{O}(A_{\mathbb{C}},\sigma_{\mathbb{C}}))

\displaystyle\to\bigoplus_{i=1}^{n}H^{0}(X,K_{X}^{2d_{i}}).

Upper half-space and precompact model.
In this case, for
z\in\mathfrak{U}_{G}
and

z^{\prime}\in\mathfrak{B}_{G}
, we have

z^{-1}T^{\mathbb{C}}_{a}\mathfrak{U}_{G}\cong T^{\mathbb{C}}_{z^{\prime}}\mathfrak{B}_{G}\cong A_{\mathbb{C}}^{-\sigma_{\mathbb{C}}}.

We define the morphism

\mathcal{N}_{\mathbb{C}}^{\mathfrak{U}}\colon T^{\mathbb{C}}\mathfrak{U}\to(A_{\mathbb{C}}^{\sigma_{\mathbb{C}}})/\!\!/\mathrm{O}(A_{\mathbb{C}},\sigma_{\mathbb{C}})

as follows. For
(z,v)\in T^{\mathbb{C}}\mathfrak{U}
, there exists
g\in\mathrm{O}(A_{\mathbb{C}},\sigma_{\mathbb{C}})
such that
g.(z,v)=(1,v_{0})
, for
v_{0}\in A_{\mathbb{C}}^{-\sigma_{\mathbb{C}}}
. Let

\mathcal{N}_{\mathbb{C}}(z,v):=[v_{0}\sigma_{\mathbb{C}}(v_{0})]\in A_{\mathbb{C}}^{\sigma_{\mathbb{C}}}/\!\!/\mathrm{O}(A_{\mathbb{C}},\sigma_{\mathbb{C}}).

This is an invariant under the action of the complexified

\mathrm{Stab}_{\mathrm{O}(A_{\mathbb{C}},\sigma_{\mathbb{C}})}(z)
on
T_{z}^{\mathbb{C}}\mathfrak{U}_{G}
which is isomorphic to
\mathrm{O}(A_{\mathbb{C}},\sigma_{\mathbb{C}})
which is the complexified stabilizer of the point
1\in\mathfrak{U}_{G}
.

The construction for
\mathfrak{B}_{G}
is similar. The only difference is that we use the point
0\in\mathfrak{B}_{G}
instead of
1\in\mathfrak{U}_{G}
.

As above, the invariants of elements in
A^{\sigma_{\mathbb{C}}}_{\mathbb{C}}
under the action of
\mathrm{O}(A_{\mathbb{C}},\sigma_{\mathbb{C}})
are given by coefficients of the characteristic polynomials which are homogeneous polynomials of degrees
d\in\{d_{1},...,d_{n}\}
of the complexification of the formally real Jordan algebra
A^{\sigma}
. Thus, for each
d
there is a map defined by the polynomial of degree
d
:

\mathcal{N}_{\mathbb{C}}^{d}\colon A_{\mathbb{C}}^{\sigma_{\mathbb{C}}}\to\mathbb{C}

and now this map for each
d
induces the map

\displaystyle\hat{\mathcal{N}}_{\mathbb{C}}^{d}\colon\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{Higgs}}(X,\mathrm{O}(A_{\mathbb{C}},\sigma_{\mathbb{C}}))

\displaystyle\to H^{0}(X,K_{X}^{2d}).

This then finally induces a map

\displaystyle\hat{\mathcal{N}}_{\mathbb{C}}\colon\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{Higgs}}(X,\mathrm{O}(A_{\mathbb{C}},\sigma_{\mathbb{C}}))

\displaystyle\to\bigoplus_{i=1}^{n}H^{0}(X,K_{X}^{2d_{i}}).

12.4.
Hitchin–Kostant–Rallis section for the real groups
\mathrm{Sp}_{2}(A,\sigma)
and
\mathrm{O}_{(1,1)}(A,\sigma)

We first recall the construction of a Hitchin–Kostant–Rallis section for real reductive groups from
[
12
]
.

Let
G
be a real reductive group, and
K\subseteq G
a maximal compact subgroup. Let
\theta:\mathfrak{g}\to\mathfrak{g}
be a Cartan involution that induces the Cartan decomposition

\mathfrak{g}=\mathfrak{k}\oplus\mathfrak{m},

where
\mathfrak{k}
is the Lie algebra of
K
. Denote by
\mathfrak{a}\subseteq\mathfrak{m}
the maximal abelian subalgebra and
W(\mathfrak{a})
(resp.
W(\mathfrak{a}^{\mathbb{C}})
) the restricted Weyl group with respect to
\mathfrak{a}
(resp.
\mathfrak{a}^{\mathbb{C}}
). Let
\widehat{\mathfrak{g}}\subseteq\mathfrak{g}^{\mathbb{C}}
be a maximal split subalgebra (see
[
12
, Definition 2.6]
), and
\widehat{G}_{0}\subseteq G
the associated maximal connected split subgroup. The restriction of
\theta
on
\widehat{\mathfrak{g}}
, which is denoted as
\widehat{\theta}
, gives
\widehat{\mathfrak{g}}=\widehat{\mathfrak{k}}\oplus\widehat{\mathfrak{m}}
. This induces the maximal compact subgroup
\widehat{K}_{0}\subseteq\widehat{G}_{0}
.

Now we assume
(G,K,\theta)
is
strongly reductive
, that is,
G
acts by inner automorphism on
\mathfrak{g}^{\mathbb{C}}
via the adjoint representation. Then, it follows from
[
12
, Section 4]
that

(1)

W(\mathfrak{a})=N_{K}(\mathfrak{a})/Z_{K}(\mathfrak{a})
and
W(\mathfrak{a}^{\mathbb{C}})=N_{K^{\mathbb{C}}}(\mathfrak{a}^{\mathbb{C}})/Z_{K^{\mathbb{C}}}(\mathfrak{a}^{\mathbb{C}})
;

(2)

\mathfrak{m}^{\mathbb{C}}/\!\!/K^{\mathbb{C}}\cong\mathfrak{a}^{\mathbb{C}}/W(\mathfrak{a}^{\mathbb{C}})\cong\widehat{\mathfrak{m}}^{\mathbb{C}}/\!\!/\widehat{K}_{0}
.

Moreover, for any real reductive
(G,K,\theta)
, its maximal connected split subgroup
(\widehat{G}_{0},\widehat{K}_{0},\widehat{\theta})
is strongly reductive
[
12
, Proposition 3.25]
.

For the moduli space
\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{Higgs}}(X,G)
of polystable
G
-Higgs bundles (of degree
0
) on
X
, the Chevalley morphism

\chi:\mathfrak{m}^{\mathbb{C}}\to\mathfrak{m}^{\mathbb{C}}/\!\!/K^{\mathbb{C}}\cong\mathfrak{a}^{\mathbb{C}}/W(\mathfrak{a}^{\mathbb{C}})

gives rise to the Hitchin morphism

h_{G}:\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{Higgs}}(X,G)\longrightarrow B(X,G):=H^{0}(X,\mathfrak{a}^{\mathbb{C}}/W(\mathfrak{a}^{\mathbb{C}})\otimes K_{X}).

An element
x\in\mathfrak{m}^{\mathbb{C}}
is
regular
if the dimension of
\mathfrak{c}_{\mathfrak{m}^{\mathbb{C}}}(x):=\mathrm{Ker}(\mathrm{ad}(x))\subseteq\mathfrak{m}^{\mathbb{C}}
is minimal, that is, if
\dim(\mathfrak{c}_{\mathfrak{m}^{\mathbb{C}}}(x))=\dim(\mathfrak{a}^{\mathbb{C}})
. Denote by
\mathfrak{m}^{\mathbb{C}}_{\mathrm{reg}}
the set of regular elements in
\mathfrak{m}^{\mathbb{C}}
. Now we fix an injective morphism
\mathfrak{sl}_{2}(\mathbb{C})\hookrightarrow\mathfrak{g}^{\mathbb{C}}
so that the image
\mathfrak{s}^{\mathbb{C}}
is normal (i.e.
\dim(\mathfrak{s}^{\mathbb{C}}\cap\mathfrak{k}^{\mathbb{C}})=1
and
\dim(\mathfrak{s}^{\mathbb{C}}\cap\mathfrak{m}^{\mathbb{C}})=2
) and principal (i.e.
\mathfrak{s}^{\mathbb{C}}
is generated by
\{e,f,x\}
with
e,f\in\mathfrak{m}^{\mathbb{C}}
are regular nilpotent, and
x\in\mathfrak{k}^{\mathbb{C}}
is semisimple).

Then the affine subspace
f+\mathfrak{c}_{\mathfrak{m}^{\mathbb{C}}}(e)\subseteq\mathfrak{m}^{\mathbb{C}}
is contained in
\mathfrak{m}^{\mathbb{C}}_{\mathrm{reg}}
and moreover, the Chevalley morphism
\chi
restricts on
f+\mathfrak{c}_{\mathfrak{m}^{\mathbb{C}}}(e)
inducing the isomorphism

f+\mathfrak{c}_{\mathfrak{m}^{\mathbb{C}}}(e)\cong\mathfrak{a}^{\mathbb{C}}/W(\mathfrak{a}^{\mathbb{C}}).

The inverse to
\chi
on
f+\mathfrak{c}_{\mathfrak{m}^{\mathbb{C}}}(e)
defines a section, called the
Kostant–Rallis
section and denoted as
s_{\mathrm{KR}}
(see
[
12
, Theorem 4.9]
).

Globally, for each choice of a square root of
K_{X}
, the Kostant–Rallis section
s_{\mathrm{KR}}
is sufficient to define special global sections of the Hitchin morphism
h_{G}
. Such sections are called
Hitchin–Kostant–Rallis sections
and are denoted as
s_{\mathrm{HKR}}
. In
[
12
]
, the authors show that there exist
N
inequivalent Hitchin–Kostant–Rallis sections of the Hitchin morphism
h_{G}
that factor through
\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{Higgs}}(X,\widehat{G}_{0})
, where
N
is the number of cosets in
\mathrm{Ad}(G)^{\theta}/\mathrm{Ad}(K)
. In particular, when
G
is complex and
\widehat{G}_{0}=G_{\mathrm{split}}
, the split real form of
G
, then these sections are the Hitchin sections (that is, sections of
h_{G}
that factor through
\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{Higgs}}(X,G_{\mathrm{split}})
).

One also has the following result:

Proposition 12.6
.

[
15
, Proposition 1.2.8]

Any connected reductive Lie group contained in a complex reductive Lie group is strongly reductive.

In view of this proposition, as well as Propositions
2.36
and
2.38
, we immediately deduce the following:

Proposition 12.7
.

Let
(A,\sigma)
be a Hermitian algebra. Then, the group
\mathrm{Sp}_{2}(A,\sigma)
is strongly reductive. Moreover, if
A^{\times}
is connected, then the group
\mathrm{O}_{(1,1)}(A,\sigma)
is strongly reductive.

One therefore obtains a Hitchin morphism and Hitchin–Kostant–Rallis sections for the moduli spaces of polystable
\mathrm{Sp}_{2}(A,\sigma)
-Higgs bundles and of polystable
\mathrm{O}_{(1,1)}(A,\sigma)
-Higgs bundles from the theory summarized above. Specifying the algebra
A
and the involution
\sigma
in each case, one can obtain a detailed description of the Hitchin base and the Hitchin–Kostant–Rallis section. We demonstrate this in a basic example below.

12.5.
Example: Hitchin base and Hitchin section for the case
G=\mathrm{Sp}_{4}(\mathbb{R})
.

We now see explicitly how the Hitchin base and the Hitchin section can be interpreted in the case when
G=\mathrm{Sp}_{4}(\mathbb{R})
in the model
\mathfrak{C}_{G}
of all complex structures on
A^{2}
, for
A=\mathrm{Mat}_{2}(\mathbb{R})
, following our analysis in Sections
12.2
and
11.2.1
.

Remember that in its classical interpretation, for the fibration

h\colon\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{Higgs}}(X,\mathrm{Sp}_{4}(\mathbb{R}))\to\mathcal{B},

the Hitchin base is the space

\mathcal{B}=H^{0}(X,K_{X}^{2})\bigoplus H^{0}(X,K_{X}^{4})

of quadratic and quartic holomorphic differentials over the Riemann surface
X
. Moreover, the points in the Hitchin section for the space
\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{Higgs}}(X,\mathrm{Sp}_{4}(\mathbb{R}))
are triples
(V,\beta,\gamma)
in the sense of Definition
11.3
with

(12.8)

V=K^{\frac{3}{2}}\oplus K^{-\frac{1}{2}},\hskip 10.00002pt\beta=\begin{pmatrix}q_{4}&q_{2}\\
q_{2}&1\end{pmatrix}\hskip 10.00002pt\text{and}\hskip 10.00002pt\gamma=\begin{pmatrix}0&1\\
1&0\end{pmatrix},

where
q_{2}\in H^{0}(X,K_{X}^{2})
and
q_{4}\in H^{0}(X,K_{X}^{4})
(see
[
7
, Table 1]
).

Following Section
12.2
, the Hitchin morphism

\displaystyle\hat{\mathcal{N}}_{\mathbb{C}}\colon\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{Higgs}}(X,\mathrm{Sp}_{4}(\mathbb{C}))

\displaystyle\to H^{0}(X,K_{X}^{2})\bigoplus H^{0}(X,K_{X}^{4})

when using the quaternionic structures model, is a map to the invariant polynomials of
T_{J_{\mathbb{C}}}^{\mathbb{C}}\mathfrak{C}
given by the traces
\mathrm{Tr}(L^{2}_{\mathbb{C}})
and
\mathrm{Tr}(L^{4}_{\mathbb{C}})
, for the
\mathbb{C}
-linear operator

L_{\mathbb{C}}\colon A^{2}_{\mathbb{C}}\to A^{2}_{\mathbb{C}}
, which in matrix presentation with respect to the basis
v=(v_{+},v_{-})
has the form
\begin{pmatrix}0&a_{-}\\
a_{+}&0\end{pmatrix}
. Here the morphisms
a_{+}\colon l_{+}\to l_{-}
and
a_{-}\colon l_{-}\to l_{+}
of the isotropic
A_{\mathbb{C}}
-lines
l_{-}
and
l_{+}
spanned by the eigenvectors
v_{-}
and
v_{+}
respectively form a basis of a complex structure
J_{\mathbb{C}}

(cf. Section
11.2.1
).

Under the 1-1 correspondence between the data
(V,V^{*},\beta,\gamma)
and
(l_{-},l_{+},a_{+},a_{-})
for an
\mathrm{Sp}_{4}(\mathbb{R})
-Higgs bundle, we now have that in the Hitchin section for the quaternionic structures model lie tuples
(l_{-},l_{+},a_{+},a_{-})
, where
l_{+}
and
l_{-}
are dual isotropic lines with respect to the form
\omega_{\mathbb{C}}
that correspond to the bundles
V=K^{\frac{3}{2}}\oplus K^{-\frac{1}{2}}
and
V^{*}=K^{-\frac{3}{2}}\oplus K^{\frac{1}{2}}
respectively, while the morphisms
a_{+}
and
a_{-}
have matrix presentation

a_{-}=\begin{pmatrix}q_{4}&q_{2}\\
q_{2}&1\end{pmatrix}\hskip 10.00002pt\text{and}\hskip 10.00002pta_{+}=\begin{pmatrix}0&1\\
1&0\end{pmatrix}.

Using the diffeomorphisms
F_{\mathfrak{C}_{G},\mathfrak{P}_{G}^{\pm}}
,
F_{\mathfrak{P}_{G}^{\pm},\mathfrak{U}_{G}^{\pm}}
and
F_{\mathfrak{P}_{G}^{+},\mathfrak{B}_{G}}
, one can now obtain the description of the Higgs data in the Hitchin section in the projective space models, the half-space models and the precompact model; these maps were studied in detail in Section
6.6
for the case when
G=\mathrm{Sp}_{2n}(\mathbb{R})
.

Appendix A
General definition

Let
G
be a Lie group and
K
be a Lie subgroup of
G
. Let
X
be a surface of negative Euler characteristic, and let
\pi\colon\tilde{X}\to X
be its universal covering.

Choose a principal
K
-bundle
p\colon E_{K}\to X
and let
\tilde{E}_{K}=\pi^{*}E_{K}\to\tilde{X}
be the pull-back bundle of
E_{K}
over
\tilde{X}
. Since
\tilde{X}
is a contractible manifold, the bundle
\tilde{E}_{K}
is topologically trivial. For every
\tilde{x}\in\tilde{X}
, the fiber
\tilde{E}_{K}|_{\tilde{x}}
is diffeomorphic to
K
.

As
K
is a subgroup of
G
, we can identify
\tilde{E}_{K}|_{\tilde{x}}
with
g_{\tilde{x}}K
for some
g_{\tilde{x}}\in G
. Such an identification is equivalent to a choice of a smooth map
f\colon\tilde{X}\to\mathcal{X}_{G}:=G/K
. The map
f
can be chosen completely free. For instance, in the classical definition of principal bundles,
g_{\tilde{x}}=1
, for all
\tilde{x}\in\tilde{X}
.

The bundle
E_{K}
is the quotient bundle of
\tilde{E}_{K}
under certain equivalence relation
\sim
, which identifies for every
\tilde{x}\in\tilde{X}
and every
\gamma\in\pi_{1}(X)
a point
(\tilde{x},k)
where
k\in\tilde{E}_{K}|_{\tilde{x}}=g_{\tilde{x}}K
with some point
(\gamma\tilde{x},k^{\prime})
for a
k^{\prime}\in\tilde{E}_{K}|_{\gamma\tilde{x}}=g_{\gamma\tilde{x}}K
, i.e.
\sim
provides a smooth map

\rho\colon\tilde{X}\times\pi_{1}(X)\to G,\text{ such that }\rho(\tilde{x},\gamma)k=k^{\prime}.

In particular,
\rho(\tilde{x},\gamma)g_{\tilde{x}}K=g_{\gamma\tilde{x}}K
. This map is “homomorphism-like” in the following sense:

\rho(\tilde{x},1)=1\text{ for all }\tilde{x}\in\tilde{X},

\rho(\tilde{x},\gamma_{2}\gamma_{1})=\rho(\gamma_{1}\tilde{x},\gamma_{2})\rho(\tilde{x},\gamma_{1})\text{ for all }\tilde{x}\in\tilde{X},\gamma_{1},\gamma_{2}\in\pi_{1}(X).

The map
f
is
\rho
-equivariant. Indeed:

f(\gamma\tilde{x})=g_{\gamma\tilde{x}}K,

\rho(\tilde{x},\gamma)f(\tilde{x})=\rho(\tilde{x},\gamma)g_{\tilde{x}}K=g_{\gamma\tilde{x}}K.

Notice that if
\rho
does not depend on
\tilde{x}
, then it is a group homomorphism in the usual sense, thus it canonically defines via the Riemann–Hilbert correspondence a flat
G
-bundle
E_{G}=(\tilde{X}\times G)/\sim
with
E_{K}\subset E_{G}
, for
\sim
the equivalence relation described above. However, the bundle
E_{G}
is also well-defined in general. It depends on the smooth map
\rho
and is not flat in general.

Similarly to the proof of Proposition
9.5
, the choice of a principal
K
-bundle
E_{K}
and the choice of the smooth map
f\colon\tilde{X}\to\mathcal{X}_{G}
thus give a bundle isomorphism for the induced pull-back bundles over
\tilde{X}

\tilde{E}_{K}(\mathfrak{m})\cong f^{*}_{\mathcal{X}}T\mathcal{X},

which is
\rho
-equivariant. This isomorphism descends to
X
, thus providing an identification between
E_{K}(\mathfrak{m})
and the quotient of
f^{*}_{\mathcal{X}}T\mathcal{X}
modulo the action by
\rho
. By a slight abuse of notation, we will still denote the latter by
f^{*}_{\mathcal{X}}T\mathcal{X}
to mean the principal bundle over
X
.

The discussion above leads to the following definition for a general
G
-Higgs bundle:

Definition A.1
.

Let
E_{K}
be a principal
K
-bundle over
X
. Let
f\colon\tilde{X}\to\mathcal{X}_{G}
be a smooth map which is equivariant with respect to the homomorphism-like map
\rho\colon\tilde{X}\times\pi_{1}(X)\to G
induced by the principal bundle
E_{K}
and the map
f
as above. Choose a holomorphic structure
\bar{\partial}_{E_{K^{\mathbb{C}}}}
to equip the bundle
E_{K^{\mathbb{C}}}
. Then, a
G
-Higgs bundle
is a pair
(E,\varphi)
where
\left(E_{K^{\mathbb{C}}},\bar{\partial}_{E_{K^{\mathbb{C}}}}\right)
is a holomorphic principal
K^{\mathbb{C}}
-bundle over
X
and
\varphi
is a section of the bundle
f^{*}_{\mathcal{X}}T^{\mathbb{C}}\mathcal{X}
with
\bar{\partial}_{E_{K^{\mathbb{C}}}}\varphi=0
.

Note that the choice of
f
guarantees the identification
\tilde{E}_{K}(\mathfrak{m})\cong f^{*}_{\mathcal{X}}T\mathcal{X}
over
\tilde{X}
. Thus, it does not impose any restriction on the holomorphic structure of a
G
-Higgs bundle.

Remark A.2
.

According to this definition, polystability of a
G
-Higgs bundle refers to the existence of a smooth map
\rho
as above being a group homomorphism and the existence of a classifying map
f
being harmonic in the sense of Section
9.1
. Note also that this does not restrict only to the cases when
G=\mathrm{Sp}_{2}(A,\sigma)
.
